Down the street, a school bus stopped at a red light. On the bus, a boy in a hoodie suddenly sat up straight. The hair on his arms was standing up. His neck was tingling.

"Ned," whispered Peter Parker. "I need you to make a distraction."

Ned looked out the window at the giant spinning donut ship. "What kind of distraction?"

"Any kind."

Ned stood up in the middle of the bus and yelled, "OH MY GOSH, EVERYBODY LOOK, THERE'S A GIANT SPACESHIP!"

Every single kid on the bus ran to the windows. Peter slipped out the emergency exit. Ten seconds later, Spider-Man was swinging down the street.

"Mr. Stark!" said Peter, landing next to Tony. "Hi! Hey! Hi, Tim! Hi, Lindsey! Hi, guys! What's going on? Is that a spaceship?"

"Hey, kid," said Tony. "Glad you're here."

"Really?" said Peter, very surprised.

"Really," said Tony. "Get everybody off the street. Fast."

So the family went to work.

Lindsey looked at the street. She looked at the buildings. She looked at her map of New York, the one she'd been carrying since the Battle of New York.

"The subway station at Washington Square," she said. "It's close. It's deep. Same as last time. Get everyone there."

Lainey took off running. "SUBWAY STATION! THIS WAY! FOLLOW ME!" People followed the little girl, faster than they'd ever run.

Sydney found a man whose car was stuck under a fallen street sign. She saw exactly where to push. She called Peter over. Peter lifted the sign with one hand. The man drove away, waving and yelling thank you.

And Tim stood on the steps of the subway station, waving everyone down into it. "Keep going! Keep going! Hold hands! Nobody gets left behind!" When a big piece of metal fell off the donut ship and came crashing toward the stairs, Tim grabbed a car door that had come off in the battle and held it up over his head like a shield.

The metal hit the car door. CLANG! It bounced off.

Tim stood there, holding the car door, very surprised that it worked.

"Nice shield, Dad!" yelled Lainey.

"I've been watching Steve," said Tim.

But up on the street, Ebony Maw was still floating. He wasn't fighting. He was waiting. And when Doctor Strange turned to help Wong, Ebony Maw lifted his long thin fingers.

Silver strings shot out of the air. Thin and shiny, like spider silk, but strong as steel. They wrapped around Strange's arms. Around his legs. Around his whole body. The cape tried to pull him away, but there were too many strings.

"You will come with me," whispered Ebony Maw. "And you will give the stone to Thanos."

"Never," said Strange.

"We shall see," said Maw.

A beam of light came down from the giant donut ship. Strange floated up into it, wrapped in silver strings. And Maw floated up after him.

"STEPHEN!" yelled Wong.

"STRANGE!" yelled Tony.

Paisley ran into the street. "No! Not Stephen! You can't take him!"

But they were already gone, up into the ship. The giant metal donut began to rise. Up, up, up, past the buildings, toward the clouds.

Tony looked at the ship. Then he looked back at the family. And at Bruce, sitting on the curb.

"I'm going after him," said Tony.

"Tony," said Lindsey. "That ship's going to space."

"I know," said Tony. "But Paisley's right. Nobody gets left behind. Not even the cranky wizard." He took a breath. "Bruce. Call Steve. I don't care that we haven't talked in forever. Call him. Tell him everything."

Bruce nodded.

"Tim," said Tony. He walked over and put one metal hand on Tim's shoulder. "Look after everybody. You're good at that."

Tim swallowed hard. "You come back. You hear me?"

Tony smiled. "I'll be back for spaghetti. Sunday, right?"

"Sunday," said Tim.

Then Tony's suit closed over his face, and he flew straight up after the donut ship.

And right behind him, swinging off the side of a building and shooting a web at the bottom of the ship, went a very small red-and-blue figure.

"PETER!" yelled Lindsey.

"I'll be fine!" yelled Peter, holding onto his web as the ship lifted him into the sky. "Don't tell May! Actually, maybe tell May! No! Don't tell May!"

And then the ship was gone. Into the clouds. Into the sky. Into space.

The street was very quiet.

Paisley's star pin beeped. A tiny blue picture of Peter popped up.

"It's really high up here," said Peter, a little breathless. "And kind of cold. Also, I think I'm in space?"

"Peter," said Tim, pressing his own pin. "We're here. We'll always pick up. Anytime. Okay? Anytime."

"Okay," said Peter. "Thanks, Tim."

The picture blinked out.

At the same time, far away, in a quiet city in Scotland, two more of Thanos's children had come for the yellow stone.

Vision and Wanda had been walking together by a river, talking about books, when the attack came. The two children of Thanos were tall and fast and fierce. One was a woman with a long sharp spear. The other was a man with a pointy face and a glowing ax.

They hurt Vision. Not too badly. But badly enough that he couldn't fly.

Wanda stood over him with her red magic swirling, holding them back. But there were two of them, and only one of her.

And then, out of the dark, came three shadows.

The first one moved like a cat, with two batons that crackled with electricity. The second one swooped down on silver metal wings. And the third one stepped out from behind a train, holding up a round shield.

Natasha. Sam. Steve.

The two children of Thanos looked at them.

"We don't want to fight you," said Steve calmly.

"Then don't," said the woman with the spear.

"We don't want to," said Steve. "But we will."

They did. And it was over very quickly. Thanos's children ran away into the sky.

Steve's star pin beeped. A tiny picture of Tim popped up in the air.

"Steve," said Tim. "Bruce is here. Strange has been taken. Tony and Peter went after him. Into space."

Steve's face went very still. "We're coming. Where are you?"

"Bring Vision," said Lindsey, leaning into the pin. "We're going to the Compound."

The Avengers Compound was quiet and dark when they got there. Rhodey was waiting at the front door in his War Machine suit.

Then the jet landed on the lawn. The door opened.

Steve walked out first. His beard had grown longer. His uniform was dusty. He looked tired. He was carrying his shield on his arm. And when he saw the family, his whole face changed. He ran across the grass and hugged every single one of them, one at a time.

Then Natasha. Then Sam. Then Wanda, helping Vision walk. Vision had a crack in his forehead, right next to his yellow stone. But he smiled when he saw Sydney.

"Hello, Sydney," he said. "I followed rule number six. When I didn't know what to do, I asked a friend."

"Which friend?" asked Sydney.

"You," said Vision.

Then Natasha saw Bruce.

They stood there and looked at each other for a long moment. Neither of them said anything. Then Natasha walked up and hugged him. A quick, tight hug.

"Hi," said Bruce.

"Hi," said Natasha. "You were gone a really long time."

"I know," said Bruce. "I'm sorry."

Everyone gathered in the big meeting room. Steve. Natasha. Sam. Rhodey. Bruce. Wanda. Vision. Wong, who came through a portal holding a cup of tea. And the family.

Lindsey laid her map on the table. Front side up. LOKI = TROUBLE. THANOS = WANTS ALL SIX STONES. Then she flipped it over. The list of stones.

"Here's what we know," said Lindsey. "Thanos has two stones. Power and Space." She tapped them. "Thor is lost somewhere in space. Strange is on a ship with the Time Stone. Tony and Peter went after him. And Thanos's children came for Vision's stone. They'll come back."

"He'll come too," said Bruce. "Thanos. Himself. He wants every single one."

"So we have to keep Vision's stone safe," said Steve. "No matter what."

"But how?" asked Rhodey. "We can't just hide it. He'll find it."

"We could destroy it," said Wanda quietly. "My magic is strong enough."

Everyone looked at Vision.

"If you destroy the stone," said Bruce slowly, "it would destroy Vision, too. The stone is what keeps him alive. It's like his heart."

Wanda's face crumpled. "No. Then no. Absolutely not."

The room got very quiet.

Then Sydney stood up on her chair. She opened her notebook. She flipped back, back, back, to a page from Wakanda. There, in big letters, underlined twice, with a star next to it:

VIBRANIUM HEART FOR VISION???

"Shuri," said Sydney. "Shuri said she could build him a new heart. Out of vibranium. So he could live, even without the stone. Then we could take the stone out, and Vision would be okay."

Everyone stared at her.

Sydney held up her wrist. The black kimoyo bracelet. "Push the third bead," she said. "Anytime, she said. Day or night."

She pushed the third bead.

A tiny glowing picture of Shuri popped up over the bracelet. She was in her pajamas, with her hair wrapped in a scarf, and she looked very sleepy.

"Sydney?" said Shuri, yawning. "It's the middle of the night. What's wrong?"

"Shuri," said Sydney. "It's about Vision's heart. It's time."

Shuri sat straight up. Her eyes went wide awake. "Bring him to Wakanda," she said. "Bring him now. I'll wake up my brother."

Steve looked at Sydney. Then he looked at Tim and Lindsey.

"I don't know what we'd do without you guys," he said.

"Good thing you'll never have to find out," said Lindsey.

That night, the jet flew south, toward Wakanda. Everybody was inside. Steve and Natasha and Sam and Rhodey and Bruce and Wanda and Vision and Wong and the whole family.

Paisley fell asleep with her head on Bruce's arm.

Lainey fell asleep holding her silver horseshoe from Sleipnir.

Sydney stayed awake, drawing. She drew a picture of Vision with a heart made of shiny vibranium. She drew Shuri in her lab. She drew a little chain of people, holding hands.

Lindsey sat at the window, looking at her map by the light of the stars.

Tim sat down beside her. "You okay?"

"I'm worried," said Lindsey. "About Tony. About Peter. About Strange. About Thor, way out there somewhere in space."

Tim took her hand. "Then we'll find them," he said. "That's what we do. Remember?"

Lindsey looked at him. Then she smiled a small smile. She turned the map over to the front. And under everything else, she wrote:

FIND TONY. FIND PETER. FIND STEPHEN. FIND THOR.

And at the bottom, in letters bigger than any of the others:

WE WILL FIND EVERYONE.

Far away, out in the dark of space, a little blue light on a spaceship called the Milano started beeping. It was a special light. Rocket had built it, a long time ago, just in case. It only went off when one of the family's star pins was talking about something very, very important.

In the pilot's seat, Peter Quill looked at it and frowned.

"Guys," he said. "The family's calling."

## Chapter 16: Rescuing Gamora

The Milano landed in Wakanda early the next morning, right on the big green lawn in front of Shuri's lab.

It was more beat up than ever. One wing had a new dent. The windows were scratched. Smoke puffed out of the back. And painted on the side, in big messy letters, someone had added a new word under MILANO: GUARDIANS.

The door hissed open.

Out jumped Peter Quill, in his long red coat. Out stomped Drax. Out walked a tall teenage tree with his arms crossed, playing a handheld video game and not looking up. Out came a raccoon in an orange jumpsuit.

And out came someone else. Someone huge and blond, with a red cape that was ripped at the bottom, and a very tired, very happy face.

"THOR!" screamed all three girls.

They ran across the grass so fast that Lainey got there first by a mile. She jumped. Thor caught her. Then he caught Sydney. Then he caught Paisley. Then Tim and Lindsey got there, and Thor tried to hug all five of them at once, and they all fell over in a big pile on the grass.

"My friends!" boomed Thor from the bottom of the pile. "My dear, dear friends!"

"Where WERE you?" said Lainey.

"In space," said Thor. "Floating. For quite a while. Then I hit something."

"He hit our windshield," said Rocket. "Splat. Like a big blond bug."

"It was a very lucky splat," said Thor.

The tall teenage tree looked up from his video game for one second. "I am Groot," he said, in a grumpy voice.

"He says hi," said Paisley, getting up off the grass. She ran over and hugged him around the middle. "Groot! You got so BIG!"

"I am Groot," muttered Groot, but he patted her crown with one twiggy hand.

"He's a teenager now," said Rocket. "He's going through a phase. Don't take it personally. He hasn't said a nice word to anybody in months."

"He just said I was his favorite person on Earth," said Paisley.

Rocket stared at her. Then he stared at Groot. Groot shrugged and went back to his video game, but his bark cheeks turned a tiny bit pink.

Then the last person came down the ramp.

She was small, with big black eyes and two long antennas on her head that bobbed when she walked. She wore a green outfit and had a gentle, curious face.

"Hello," she said shyly. "I'm Mantis."

"Mantis can feel what people are feeling," said Quill. "If she touches you."

"May I?" asked Mantis, holding out her hand to Paisley.

Paisley held out hers. Mantis touched it with one finger. Her antennas lit up and glowed.

Mantis gasped. She pulled her hand back and held it against her chest.

"What?" said Quill. "What do you feel?"

"So much love," whispered Mantis. Her eyes were shining. "So, so much. It is like standing in the sun."

Paisley beamed.

But Quill wasn't smiling. Now that everyone was together, his face looked worried. Very worried. Lindsey saw it right away.

"Peter," she said. "Where's Gamora?"

Quill swallowed. He looked at the ground.

"Thanos has her," he said.

Everyone sat on the grass in a big circle. Shuri came out of her lab. T'Challa came, and Okoye, and Steve and Natasha and Bruce. Vision and Wanda stood at the edge, holding hands.

Quill told the story.

"We went to Knowhere," he said. "To warn the Collector. Because the Reality Stone was there. But Thanos got there first. The Collector hid in a big cabinet, so he was okay, I guess. But Thanos took the red stone."

Lindsey turned her map over. 2. Reality Stone (red). She crossed out "The Collector has it" and wrote: THANOS HAS IT.

Now there were three big circles on the list.

"And then he took Gamora," said Quill. His voice cracked. "He just grabbed her and opened a portal and they were gone. He took her up to his giant ship."

"Why does he want her?" asked Sydney quietly.

"Because Gamora knows a secret," said Quill. "A long time ago, she found out where the Soul Stone is. The sixth one. The last one. Nobody else knows. And Thanos wants her to tell him."

"She won't tell him," said Drax firmly. "She is very stubborn. It is one of her best qualities."

"No," said Quill. "She won't. But he's going to keep trying until she does."

Then Quill looked across the circle at Lindsey.

"Before he took her," said Quill, "she told me something. Weeks ago. She said, 'If my father ever takes me, find Lindsey. She can find anything. Anywhere. Even in the dark.'"

Lindsey sat very still.

"So I'm asking," said Quill. "Please. Help me find her."

Lindsey looked at Tim. Tim looked back at her. They didn't even have to talk. They just knew.

"We'll come," said Lindsey.

"All of us," said Tim.

Then Thor stood up.

"I must go somewhere else," he said. "I need a new weapon. One strong enough to stop Thanos. There is a place called Nidavellir. It is a giant forge, built around a dying star, where the dwarves make the greatest weapons in the universe. My hammer was made there."

"I'll fly him," said Rocket. "Groot and me. We'll take the little ship."

Paisley jumped up. She ran to her backpack. She dug all the way to the bottom. And she pulled out something heavy, wrapped in her sweater.

She brought it to Thor and put it in his hands.

It was the pieces of Mjolnir. All of them. Every single piece. She had carried them in her backpack since the cliff in Norway.

Thor stared down at them.

"If the dwarves made her," said Paisley, "maybe they can fix her."

Thor didn't say anything for a long time. Then he knelt down in front of her. He wrapped her small hands around the bundle, and then wrapped his big hands around hers.

"Princess Paisley," he said. "When I come back, I promise. I will bring her back to you."

Then Thor, Rocket, and Groot climbed into a little ship and zoomed off into the sky.

The Milano was crowded. Quill, Drax, and Mantis. Tim, Lindsey, and the three girls. Quill put on his music, very quietly. Nobody danced this time.

"Thanos's ship is huge," said Quill, flying fast through the stars. "It's called the Sanctuary. It's like a whole city in space. Hundreds of hallways. Thousands of rooms. We'll never find her in time."

"Yes, we will," said Lindsey.

She sat in the copilot's seat with her map, the space one she had drawn on their first trip with the Guardians. She looked at the stars. She looked at the swirling clouds of colorful space dust. She drew lines.

"He'd keep his ship somewhere safe," she said slowly. "Somewhere hidden. Somewhere nobody would come by accident." She tapped a spot on her map. A big dark area with no stars at all. "Here. In the middle of the dark part. Where nobody ever flies."

Quill steered toward it.

And there it was.

The Sanctuary.

It was gigantic. It looked like a huge metal flower, gray and black, with spiky petals that pointed out in every direction. Little lights blinked along the edges. It was so big that the Milano looked like a tiny fly next to it.

"Okay," said Quill. "We found it. Now how do we find her inside?"

Lindsey looked at the giant ship through the window for a long, long time. She looked at the lights. Most of them were white. Some of them were blue. And on one petal, near the very bottom, there were three little lights that were red.

"Red means stop," said Lindsey. "Red means don't come in. If I were keeping someone locked up, I'd put red lights on the door." She pointed. "There. The red ones. That's where she is."

Quill looked at her. "Are you sure?"

"No," said Lindsey. "But I'm almost always right."

"She is," said Tim. "It's kind of annoying."

The Milano snuck up to the bottom of the ship as quietly as it could. Quill parked it right underneath the red lights, in a dark corner where the cameras couldn't see.

They climbed out through a small hatch and into a long gray hallway. It was cold. It smelled like metal. Somewhere far away, something went BOOM, BOOM, BOOM, like a giant heart.

Then two guards came around the corner.

They were big and gray, with long arms and sharp teeth. They saw the family. They opened their mouths to shout.

Mantis stepped forward. She reached out and touched both guards on the arm.

"Sleep," she said softly.

The guards' eyes drooped. They yawned. They lay down on the floor right there in the hallway and started to snore.

"Whoa," whispered Lainey. "Can you do that to Dad when he snores?"

"Hey!" whispered Tim.

They tiptoed past. Down the hallway. Around a corner. And then another guard came walking right toward them.

There was nowhere to hide. Mantis was too far away. Everyone froze.

Except Drax. Drax stepped to the side of the hallway. He stood perfectly still. He didn't blink. He didn't breathe. He stood so still, he looked like a statue.

The guard walked right past him. He didn't even look. He walked right past the whole family, too, because he was busy looking at Drax and thinking, what a weird statue. Then he turned the corner and was gone.

Drax let out his breath.

"I told you," he whispered. "I am so still that I am invisible."

"That," whispered Sydney, "actually worked."

"Of course it did," said Drax.

They found a big door with three red lights over it. It was locked. There was a keypad next to it, with lots of strange buttons in a language nobody could read.

"Can anyone read this?" asked Quill.

Sydney stepped up. She looked at the keypad. She looked at the buttons. Then she took off her kimoyo bracelet and pressed one of the beads against the keypad.

The bead glowed. The keypad glowed. Strange letters scrolled across a tiny screen.

"Shuri said this bead can read any lock," said Sydney. "She tested it on her mom's cookie jar." She took a tiny screwdriver out of her pocket, the one Rocket had given her. She opened a little panel under the keypad. Inside, there was a mess of wires.

"Red goes to blue," she said. "Blue goes to green." Twist, twist, twist. "And this one..." She found a little loose wire. "Is loose. Of course it's loose."

She tightened it.

The three red lights turned green. The door slid open.

Inside was a small, dark room. And standing in the middle of it, with her arms crossed, was Gamora.

She turned around. She looked at the door. She saw Quill. She saw Drax and Mantis. She saw the family.

Her eyes went wide.

"Lindsey," she breathed. "You found me."

"I told you we'd come," said Lindsey.

Gamora ran across the room and hugged her. Then she hugged Quill. Then she hugged everybody, even Drax, who said, "This is a very long hug," but didn't let go.

"Where's Thanos?" asked Tim.

"He's not here," said Gamora. "He left. He wanted me to tell him where the Soul Stone is. I wouldn't. So he went to find out another way." Her face fell. "He took my sister."

"You have a sister?" asked Paisley.

"Nebula," said Gamora. "She's here too. Somewhere. He's been keeping her locked up. When we were little, he made us fight each other all the time. We didn't like each other very much." She looked at the floor. "But she's still my sister."

Tim was already walking back out the door.

"Then we find her too," he said. "Nobody gets left behind."

Gamora stared at him. "You don't even know her."

"Doesn't matter," said Tim. "She's your family. That makes her ours too."

They found Nebula two rooms away. She was blue, with a bald head and pieces of shiny metal on her arms and face, where parts of her had been replaced with machines. She was hanging inside a big glass box with wires all over it.

She looked up when the door opened. She looked angry. Then she looked confused. Then, when she saw Gamora, she looked like she didn't know what to look like.

"Sister," said Gamora softly.

Sydney opened the glass box. Tim helped Nebula out. She was very weak. Her legs shook. She could barely stand.

So Tim picked her up. Just like he picked up the girls when they were tired.

Nebula stared at him. "What are you doing?"

"Carrying you," said Tim. "You're tired. We're going home."

"Why would you help me?" said Nebula. "You don't know me. I'm not nice. I'm not good. Nobody has ever carried me anywhere."

"Well," said Tim, walking out the door with her in his arms, "now somebody has."

Nebula didn't say anything else. But she held onto Tim's shirt very tightly the whole way back to the ship.

When they were all on the Milano, flying away from the giant ship, Gamora sat down next to Lindsey.

"I need to tell you something," said Gamora. "About the Soul Stone."

Everyone got quiet.

"It's on a planet called Vormir," said Gamora. "A cold, dark place, at the top of a tall mountain. I found it a long time ago. And I learned something about it. Something terrible." She looked at her hands. "The Soul Stone is guarded by a spirit. A Stonekeeper. And the stone will only go to someone who is willing to give up the thing they love most."

Lindsey looked at her. "And what does Thanos love most?"

Gamora was quiet for a long time.

"Me," she said finally. "In his own strange, wrong, mixed-up way. He loves me. That's why he wanted me to tell him where it was. He was going to take me there. And give me up for the stone."

Quill made a sound like someone had punched him in the stomach.

"But now you're not there," said Paisley. "You're here. With us."

"Yes," said Gamora. And slowly, she smiled. "I'm here with you. And that means Thanos can't get the Soul Stone. Not without me." She looked at Lindsey. "You didn't just find me. You stopped him from getting the last stone."

Lindsey turned her map over. At the very bottom of the list, she wrote:

6. Soul Stone (orange). On a planet called Vormir. THANOS CAN'T GET IT. Because we have Gamora.

She drew a smiley face next to it. Then she drew a heart.

Just then, Paisley's star pin beeped. A tiny picture of Peter Parker popped up. He looked dusty and tired, and his mask was half off.

"Hey," said Peter. "Hi. It's me. So, um. We're on a planet. It's orange. Everything's kind of broken. Mr. Stark is here. And Doctor Strange. We saved Doctor Strange! It was awesome! Mr. Stark made a hole in the side of the ship and the pale guy got sucked out into space and floated away, way, way away, and then we crashed." He took a breath. "But um. Doctor Strange says Thanos is coming here. Like, right now. Can you come too?"

"Where are you?" asked Lindsey.

"I don't know," said Peter. "It's called Titan. There's like, nobody here. It used to be Thanos's home, I think."

Gamora sat up very straight. "Titan," she said. "I know where that is."

"So do I," said Lindsey, already drawing it on her map.

Titan was a sad planet. The ground was orange and dusty. Giant broken buildings lay on their sides, like toys that had been knocked over. Big chunks of rock floated in the sky above, not quite falling, not quite flying.

The Milano landed in a cloud of orange dust. Tony was waiting for them, in his red and gold suit. Peter stood beside him, waving both arms over his head. And Doctor Strange stood a little ways away, with his cape fluttering and his eyes closed.

When Peter saw the family, he ran over and hugged them all. "You came! You actually came! Mr. Stark, they came!"

"I see that," said Tony. He walked over and looked at Tim. "You came all the way to space."

"Nobody gets left behind," said Tim. "You taught me that. Actually, Steve did. But you were there."

Tony laughed. Then he hugged Tim, metal suit and all. CLANK.

Doctor Strange opened his eyes. He looked very, very tired.

"Stephen," said Paisley. "Are you okay?"

"I'm fine," said Strange. "I was just looking at the future."

"How?" asked Sydney.

Strange touched the gold eye around his neck. The green stone glowed. "With the Time Stone. I looked at every way this fight could end. Millions of them."

"How many did we win?" asked Quill.

Strange was quiet for a moment. "Not many," he said. "But some." Then he looked at the family. "And in every single one where we win, they're there."

Everybody looked at Tim and Lindsey and the girls.

"Us?" said Lainey. "Really?"

"Really," said Strange. "I don't know why. I don't know how. But you're always there."

Then the air shimmered. A blue, swirling doorway opened in the sky. It was a portal. Made by the Space Stone.

And out of it stepped Thanos.

He was so big. Even bigger than Bruce had said. Taller than the Hulk. His skin was purple. His chin was huge and wrinkled. He wore gold armor on his chest and his shoulders. And on his left hand was the giant golden glove.

Three stones glowed in it now. Blue. Purple. Red.

He looked around at all of them. He didn't look angry. He looked calm. Like a grown-up looking at a bunch of kids who were up past their bedtime.

Then he saw Gamora.

His face changed.

"Daughter," he said, in a deep, rumbly voice.

Gamora stepped forward. Her hands were shaking. But her voice was steady.

"I'm not your daughter anymore," she said. "I have a new family now. They ask me what I want. You never did. Not once."

Thanos looked at her. For a moment, something like sadness flickered across his giant face.

Then it was gone.

"Then I will take what I need," he said.

The fight began.

Strange's plan was clever. Everyone had a job.

Strange made copies of himself, hundreds of them, all around Thanos, like a hall of mirrors. Tony fired every beam his suit had. Peter swung around Thanos's head and webbed his eyes shut. Quill and Drax and Nebula fought him from below. And Mantis waited for her chance.

When Thanos was tangled up in webs and magic and couldn't see, Mantis jumped onto his shoulders. She grabbed his giant head with both hands.

"SLEEP," she said.

Thanos's body went heavy. His knees bent. His eyes drooped. He was fighting it. Mantis was shaking with the effort, her antennas glowing bright.

"Hurry!" said Mantis. "He's so strong! I can't hold him long!"

"The glove!" yelled Tony. "Get the glove off!"

Peter grabbed one of Thanos's giant golden fingers. Tony grabbed another. They pulled. And pulled. The glove started to slide.

"It's coming!" yelled Peter. "It's coming off! Keep pulling!"

Lindsey grabbed the girls and pulled them behind a big broken rock. But Lainey couldn't stand still. She peeked out. And she saw it first.

"His other hand!" yelled Lainey. "His other hand is moving! He's waking up!"

Thanos's other hand twitched. Then it clenched into a fist. His eyes flickered open. Then they opened all the way.

He flung Mantis off his shoulders. He flung Peter away. He flung Tony into a rock. CRASH!

He shoved the glove back on, tight.

The red stone glowed. And suddenly, the ground under everyone's feet turned into bubbles. Thousands of shiny, floating bubbles, like the inside of a bubble bath. Everyone fell through them, tumbling and bouncing, and landed in a pile on the other side.

"Reality Stone," gasped Sydney. "He can change what's real."

Thanos stood over them all. He was breathing hard. He was a little bit hurt. But he was standing.

He walked toward Gamora.

Tim stood up.

He didn't have a suit. He didn't have a shield. He didn't have magic or webs or super strength. He just had a star pin on his shirt, right over his heart.

He stood in front of Gamora.

"No," said Tim.

Thanos looked down at him. Way, way down. "Move aside, small one."

"No," said Tim again.

Lindsey stood up next to him. She took his hand.

Then Lainey stood up and took Lindsey's hand. Then Sydney. Then Paisley. Then Quill, then Drax, then Mantis, then Nebula, who had never held anybody's hand in her whole life. They made a big chain, all of them, standing in a row in front of Gamora.

"You'll have to go through all of us," said Paisley.

Thanos looked at them. All of them. Standing there, holding hands.

For a moment, he didn't move.

Then he raised his giant golden fist. The purple stone glowed. Tony flew in front of everyone, his suit cracked and sparking. Thanos's fist came down toward him.

"WAIT!" yelled Strange.

Everyone stopped.

Strange was standing at the edge of the broken buildings. His cape was torn. His hair was messy. He was holding the gold eye in his hand. The green Time Stone glowed inside it.

"Leave them alone," said Strange. "All of them. Leave Tony. Leave Gamora. Leave everyone here. And I'll give you the stone."

"STEPHEN, NO!" yelled Tony.

Thanos looked at Strange. Then he looked at the green stone.

"No tricks?" said Thanos.

"No tricks," said Strange.

Thanos lowered his fist. He stepped back from Tony. He stepped back from the chain of people holding hands. He held out his giant golden hand.

Strange opened the eye. The green stone floated out. It drifted across the air, slowly, and settled into the golden glove with a soft click.

Four stones now. Blue. Purple. Red. Green.

Thanos looked at Gamora one more time. Then he looked at Lindsey, who was holding Tim's hand, who was holding Lainey's hand.

"You have found her a family," said Thanos quietly. "That is unfortunate."

Then he opened a blue portal and stepped through it. And he was gone.

Everybody stood very still in the orange dust.

Then Tony turned around and grabbed Strange by the cape. "Why? Why would you do that? You said you'd protect it! You had it! Why would you give it to him?"

Strange looked at Tony. Then he looked at the family.

"Paisley told me not to protect it with my life," he said. "She told me to protect it with my friends. So that's what I did. I protected my friends."

Tony let go of the cape slowly.

"Besides," said Strange, very quietly, so only Tony could hear, "it was the only way. I looked at every future. This was the only one where we can still win."

"Win how?" said Tony.

Strange shook his head. "If I tell you, it won't happen."

Tony stared at him for a long time. Then he sat down hard on a rock. "I hate magic," he said.

Paisley sat down next to Strange.

"Did you do the right thing?" she asked.

Strange looked at her. "I hope so," he said. "I think so."

"Then it's okay," said Paisley. She took his hand. His shaking hand. And she held it still.

Lindsey turned her map over and looked at the list. She crossed out "Doctor Strange keeps it" next to the Time Stone. And she wrote, very carefully:

THANOS HAS IT. (Stephen traded it for all of us.)

Now there were four circles. Only two stones left without circles.

4. Mind Stone (yellow). In Vision's forehead.
6. Soul Stone (orange). THANOS CAN'T GET IT. Because we have Gamora.

"He'll go to Earth next," said Gamora. "For the yellow stone. For Vision."

"Vision is in Wakanda," said Lindsey. "Shuri is fixing his heart right now. So she can take the stone out safely."

"Then we'd better get to Wakanda," said Quill. "Fast."

Everybody piled into the Milano. Tony. Peter. Strange. Quill and Drax and Mantis. Gamora and Nebula. And the family. It was so crowded that Peter had to sit on the ceiling, stuck there with his spider powers, waving down at everyone.

"This is the coolest seat," said Peter.

"I want the ceiling!" said Lainey.

"You don't have spider powers," said Sydney.

"I'll get some," said Lainey.

Quill turned on his music. An old song came on, with drums and guitars and happy singing. For a moment, nobody said anything.

Then Nebula, sitting stiffly in the corner, looked at Tim.

"Thank you," she said, very quietly. "For carrying me."

"Anytime," said Tim.

Nebula looked at him. She looked at the family. She looked at her sister, sitting next to Quill, with Paisley asleep in her lap.

"I think," said Nebula slowly, "that I have never had a family before."

"Well," said Tim. "Now you've got two."

Nebula looked at him for a long time. And then, for the very first time in her whole life, Nebula smiled.

## Chapter 17: The Battle of Wakanda

When the Milano landed in Wakanda, it was the middle of the night. But nobody was sleeping.

The whole city was awake. Lights glowed in every tower. Wakandan warriors in purple and red armor marched down the streets. The war rhinos stamped and snorted in their field. And at the edge of the city, a giant glowing dome of blue light covered everything, like a bubble. It was Wakanda's shield. Nothing could get through it.

At least, that's what everyone hoped.

The family ran straight to Shuri's lab.

Vision was lying on a white table in the middle of the room. His eyes were closed. Floating screens surrounded him, full of numbers and pictures. Shuri stood over him with a little glowing tool in her hand. She had dark circles under her eyes. She looked like she hadn't slept at all.

Wanda stood next to the table, holding Vision's hand.

"Sydney," said Shuri, without even looking up. "Thank goodness. I need you."

"How's it going?" asked Sydney, running to her side.

"Slowly," said Shuri. "Too slowly. The stone is connected to everything inside him. Thousands and thousands of little tiny connections. Like roots on a tree. I have to move each one, one at a time, from the stone to the new heart. If I go too fast, it won't work."

She pointed at a little shiny thing sitting in a dish next to Vision. It was silver and smooth, about the size of a plum, and shaped like a heart. Very faintly, it was glowing.

"Vibranium," said Shuri. "His new heart. It's ready. I just need to connect it."

Sydney looked at the screens. She looked at the thousands of tiny glowing lines going from the yellow stone to every part of Vision's body.

"It's like Bucky," said Sydney. "Like untangling a necklace. Except a really, really big necklace."

"Exactly," said Shuri. "So I need small hands. And a big brain."

Sydney put on a pair of Shuri's special gloves. They were too big. Shuri tapped a button, and they shrank to fit.

"Let's go," said Sydney.

While Sydney worked, the rest of the family walked down to the river with Steve and Bucky.

The moon was out. The water was quiet. Somewhere far away, the war rhinos were snorting in their field. Bucky sat on a rock and skipped stones across the river. His new vibranium arm made them skip seven, eight, nine times.

"Show off," said Steve.

"You're just jealous," said Bucky. "You could never skip a stone. Not once. Not even when we were kids."

"Is that true?" asked Lainey.

"Totally true," said Bucky. "He'd throw it, and it would just go PLUNK. Straight to the bottom. Every time. And then he'd try again. And again. For hours." He grinned. "One time he tried for so long that it got dark, and his mom came looking for us, and he made her wait while he tried one more."

"Did he ever get it?" asked Paisley.

Bucky looked at Steve. Steve looked at the river.

"No," said Bucky. "But he never stopped trying, either. That's Steve. He never, ever stops."

Steve picked up a flat stone. He looked at it. He threw it.

It skipped. Once. Twice. Then PLUNK.

"TWO!" yelled Lainey. "You got two!"

Steve stared at the water. Then he started to laugh. "All those years," he said. "All those years, and I finally got two."

Then he sat down on the rock next to Bucky, and he got quiet.

Tim sat down next to him.

"You okay?" asked Tim.

Steve didn't answer for a while. "I'm scared," he said finally. "I don't usually say that out loud. But I am. Thanos is so strong. I don't know if we can win this time."

Tim thought about it. He picked up a stone too. He threw it. It went straight to the bottom. PLUNK.

"I don't know either," said Tim. "But you taught me something. A good captain doesn't have to know he'll win. He just has to never give up. And never leave anyone behind." He looked at Steve. "I'm not going to give up. And I'm not going to leave you behind. So you don't have to be scared alone."

Steve looked at him for a long time.

"When did you get so smart?" he asked.

"I had a good teacher," said Tim.

Paisley climbed up on the rock and squeezed in between them. Lainey squeezed in next to Bucky. Lindsey sat on the grass behind them all and put her hand on Steve's shoulder.

They sat there together, by the river, under the moon, and nobody said anything else for a long time.

In the morning, everyone else got ready.

T'Challa stood at the front of the Wakandan army in his black panther suit. Okoye stood beside him with her spear. M'Baku stood on his other side with his big wooden club. Behind them stood hundreds of Wakandan warriors with shields that could make walls of blue light.

Steve was there, with a brand-new shield made of vibranium, a gift from Shuri. Natasha was there, with her electric batons. Sam was there, with his wings. Rhodey was there, in his War Machine suit.

And Bucky was there. He had his new arm now. It was shiny and silver, made of vibranium, with gold lines. He flexed his fingers and smiled at Steve.

"How do I look?" asked Bucky.

"Like you," said Steve. "Finally."

Tony and Peter and Strange were there. The Guardians were there. Gamora and Nebula stood side by side, which they had never done before.

And Bruce was there, too. Wearing the Hulkbuster.

It was Tony's giant suit of armor, the one he'd built to stop the Hulk. Now Bruce was wearing it. He was standing inside it, way up high, with his face peeking out through a window in the chest.

"The Hulk still won't come out," said Bruce. "So I'm going to fight like this. Like a regular guy in a really, really big suit."

Paisley walked up to the Hulkbuster and knocked on its giant metal leg. Knock, knock.

Bruce leaned down. "Hi, Paisley."

"Can I talk to him?" asked Paisley. "The Hulk? Inside?"

Bruce looked surprised. "I... I guess so. He can hear you. He just won't answer."

Paisley put both hands on the giant metal leg. She put her mouth close to it, like she was whispering into a seashell.

"Hulk," she said. "It's Crown Girl. I know you're scared. I know Thanos was really mean. But you're not alone. We're all here. Every single person. Nobody's going to leave you behind." She patted the metal. "Come out when you're ready. Okay? I'll be right here."

Deep inside Bruce, something rumbled. Not a roar. A grumble. Like someone saying, I heard you.

Bruce's eyes got shiny. "He heard you," he said.

"I know," said Paisley.

Lindsey stood at the top of a tall tower with Okoye, looking out over the great green plains beyond the shield. She had her map of Wakanda. She had a pair of Wakandan binoculars that could see for miles.

And she could see them coming.

Thousands of them. Coming over the hills. They were gray and fast, with too many legs and long sharp claws and big mouths full of teeth. They looked like a mix between angry dogs and giant bugs. They ran so fast, they kicked up a cloud of dust that blocked out the morning sun.

"Outriders," said Gamora, standing beside her. "My father's army. They don't think. They just run and attack."

Behind the outriders came three people.

One was a woman with a long sharp spear. One was a man with a pointy face and a glowing ax. And one was huge and rocky, with a hammer instead of a hand.

The big rocky one sneezed. ACHOO!

"He's been in Antarctica," said Lindsey. "He has a cold."

Okoye almost smiled.

The outriders ran right up to the blue shield. They crashed into it. ZAP! ZAP! ZAP! The shield buzzed and sparked. The outriders bounced off, fell down, got up, and crashed into it again.

"They can't get through!" said Lainey, standing on her tiptoes to see.

Lindsey wasn't so sure. She watched the outriders very carefully through her binoculars. Most of them were crashing into the front of the shield. But some of them weren't.

Some of them were going around to the side. And they weren't crashing. They were digging.

"Okoye," said Lindsey. "Look. On the left side. By the river. They're digging under the shield. If they dig deep enough, they'll come up on the inside."

Okoye grabbed the binoculars. She looked. Her face went hard. "You are right."

She touched the kimoyo bead on her wrist. "My king. The outriders are digging under the shield by the river. They will come up on the inside."

T'Challa's voice came back right away. "Then we will be ready for them. Thank you, Lindsey."

Down on the field, Lainey was bouncing up and down next to Tim.

"I want to help," she said. "I want to do something."

Tim looked at the outriders. Then he looked at the war rhinos, stamping in their field.

"Lainey," he said slowly. "What if the outriders came through the shield in just one place? A little door. Where we knew they'd come? And the rhinos were waiting?"

Lainey's eyes lit up.

Tim called T'Challa on his star pin. T'Challa listened. Then he smiled.

"Your family," said T'Challa, "is very good at plans."

Here was the plan.

T'Challa would open one small door in the shield. Just one. Lainey would stand right inside it. When the outriders saw her, they would chase her. She would run. And she would lead them in a big circle, right into the middle of the field, where the war rhinos and M'Baku's warriors would be waiting.

"Are you sure?" said Lindsey, holding Lainey's face in both hands. "Are you absolutely sure?"

"I'm the fastest runner anyone has ever seen," said Lainey. "You said so."

"I did say so," said Lindsey. She kissed Lainey on the forehead. "Then run, Little Rocket. Run like you've never run before."

"And come right back," said Tim. "Straight back. To us."

"Straight back," promised Lainey.

She ran to the edge of the shield. T'Challa touched his wrist. A small door opened in the blue light, just big enough for one person.

On the other side, a hundred outriders turned their heads. They saw a little girl standing in the doorway.

Lainey waved. "HI! BET YOU CAN'T CATCH ME!"

The outriders charged.

Lainey ran.

She ran faster than she'd ever run in her whole life. Faster than she ran from the Hulk's lab. Faster than she ran in the Battle of New York. Faster than she raced Sleipnir, or Steve, or Spider-Man, or Pietro. She zigged and zagged across the field. The outriders poured through the little door behind her, a big gray river of claws and teeth.

She ran past the warriors. She ran past the trees. She ran in a big wide circle, all the way around the field, with the outriders right behind her, getting closer and closer and closer.

"Almost there!" yelled Tim. "Almost there, Lainey!"

Lainey ran right into the middle of the field. The outriders followed her.

And then, from every side at once, came the rhinos.

Fifty war rhinos, as big as trucks, with armor on their backs and horns on their noses, charged out of the trees. M'Baku's warriors came behind them, shouting "HOO! HOO! HOO!" The rhinos smashed into the outriders like bowling balls into pins.

BOOM! CRASH! WHAM!

Outriders went flying everywhere.

And Lainey kept running, straight back to Tim, just like she promised. She jumped into his arms. He caught her and spun her around.

"YOU DID IT!" yelled Tim.

"I DID IT!" yelled Lainey.

Sitting on top of the biggest rhino, holding onto its armor with both hands and laughing her head off, was Paisley.

"PAISLEY!" yelled Lindsey. "HOW DID YOU GET ON A RHINO?"

"He asked me to come!" yelled Paisley. "He licked me first! That means we're friends!"

The big rhino trotted over and lowered its head so Paisley could slide down. Then it licked her one more time, from her chin to her crown, and trotted back into battle.

Okoye watched from the tower, shaking her head. "Three years," she muttered. "It took me three years."

But the outriders kept coming. There were so many of them. More and more and more poured over the hills, and more and more dug under the shield. The heroes fought. The warriors fought. The rhinos charged.

And it wasn't enough.

The outriders got through. They swarmed across the field. The Wakandan warriors got pushed back, step by step. Rhodey fell out of the sky. Bucky and Sam got surrounded. Steve was knocked down. He got back up. He got knocked down again.

Tim was on the field, helping a Wakandan soldier who had fallen. The soldier's leg was hurt. He couldn't walk. Outriders were coming.

Tim grabbed him by the arm and pulled him up and put the soldier's arm over his own shoulders.

"Come on," said Tim. "I've got you. Nobody gets left behind."

They limped across the field together. The outriders were right behind them. Tim was not fast. He was not strong. But he did not let go.

Lindsey watched from the tower, gripping the rail so hard her knuckles went white.

Then she saw something else. Something nobody on the field could see.

Two shadows were slipping through the smoke at the edge of the battle. Away from the fighting. Toward the city. They were moving very quietly, staying low, ducking behind rocks.

Lindsey grabbed the binoculars.

It was the woman with the long sharp spear. And the man with the pointy face and the glowing ax. Two of Thanos's children. And they weren't fighting anybody. They were sneaking. Straight toward Shuri's lab.

"Okoye," said Lindsey. "The spear lady and the ax man. They're not fighting. They're sneaking around the back. They're going for Vision."

Okoye didn't even ask how Lindsey knew. She touched her kimoyo bead. "Natasha. Wanda. Two of them, coming around the river side of the lab. Now."

In the lab, Wanda looked up from Vision's bed. Her eyes glowed red.

Down by the river, the two children of Thanos crept up to the back door of the lab. The woman with the spear reached for the handle.

The door opened before she touched it.

Natasha was standing there, with her batons crackling. Okoye was standing next to her, with her spear. And behind them, floating a few inches off the floor, with red magic swirling around her hands, was Wanda.

"Looking for someone?" said Natasha.

It was a very short fight.

When it was over, the two children of Thanos were tied up in a big net made of glowing purple Wakandan rope. The woman with the spear was very angry about it. The man with the ax was mostly just embarrassed.

"How did you know?" the woman with the spear hissed. "Nobody saw us."

Okoye pointed up at the tower, where a tiny figure was waving a map.

"She did," said Okoye.

Then, all at once, the sky lit up.

A beam of rainbow light crashed down into the middle of the battlefield. BOOOOOM! It hit the ground so hard that every outrider within a hundred feet flew backward into the air.

And when the light faded, three figures were standing in the middle of the crater.

A raccoon with a giant gun. A teenage tree, holding a brand new ax handle that he had grown from his own arm.

And Thor.

Lightning crackled all over him. His eyes glowed white. In one hand, he held a giant ax with a long handle and a shiny head that glowed with the light of a star. In the other hand, he held a hammer.

A square, gray hammer, with a short handle wrapped in leather. Smooth and whole. With thin lines of gold running across it, where all the pieces had been put back together.

Mjolnir.

"THOR!" screamed the whole family.

Thor raised both his weapons over his head. Lightning poured down from the sky. It hit the ax. It hit the hammer. And Thor pointed both of them at the army of outriders.

CRAAAAAAACK!

Lightning ripped across the whole battlefield. The outriders scattered like leaves in the wind. They ran. They fell. They ran away over the hills, yelping.

The Wakandan army cheered so loud that the ground shook.

Thor walked across the battlefield to where Paisley was standing with Tim. He knelt down in front of her. He held out Mjolnir.

"I made you a promise," he said. "The dwarves of Nidavellir put her back together. Every single piece you saved. Not one was missing." He set the hammer down on the grass next to her feet. "Because you picked them all up. Because you don't throw away a friend's broken things."

Paisley looked at the hammer. The gold lines shone in the sunlight, like happy little scars.

"She's even prettier now," said Paisley.

"She is," said Thor. "Broken things often are, once they're fixed."

Then he turned to the battlefield and held up his new ax. "This one is called Stormbreaker," he said. "Groot gave her a handle."

"I am Groot," said Groot proudly.

"He says it's the best handle in the universe," said Paisley.

"It is," said Rocket. "Kid's got talent."

In the lab, Sydney and Shuri were almost done.

Sydney's hands were steady. Shuri's eyes were sharp. Line by line, connection by connection, they moved all of Vision's thousands of tiny roots away from the yellow stone and into the shiny new heart.

"Three left," said Shuri.

Sydney moved one.

"Two left."

Sydney moved another.

"One left."

The whole building shook. BOOM. Dust fell from the ceiling.

Wanda looked out the window. Her face went white.

"He's here," she said.

Out on the battlefield, a blue swirling portal had opened.

And out of it stepped Thanos.

He looked bigger than ever. In his golden glove, four stones glowed. Blue. Purple. Red. Green. They hummed with so much power that the air around him rippled, like heat over a road in summer.

Everyone on the field turned to face him.

Steve ran at him first. Thanos swatted him away with the back of his hand.

Bucky ran at him next. Thanos swatted him too. Sam flew at him. Rhodey flew at him. Natasha. T'Challa. Okoye. Every single one of them got knocked down.

Every single one of them got back up.

But Thanos kept walking. Slowly. Step by step. Toward Shuri's lab.

Toward Vision.

Bruce stepped in front of him, in the giant Hulkbuster suit.

"Not today," said Bruce.

Thanos looked at the suit. "Where is your green friend?"

"He's resting," said Bruce. "You're stuck with me."

Thanos raised his glove. The blue stone glowed. And the Hulkbuster suit was suddenly upside down, stuck halfway into a rock wall a mile away.

Inside the suit, Bruce yelled, "HULK! NOW WOULD BE A GOOD TIME!"

Deep inside him, the Hulk grumbled. Not yet. Not yet. But soon.

Inside the lab, Sydney moved the very last connection.

The silver heart in Vision's chest lit up bright, bright silver.

Then it went thump.

Thump. Thump. Thump.

Vision took a big breath. His eyes opened.

Shuri reached up and gently, so gently, lifted the yellow stone out of his forehead. It came out easily, like a sticker off a page. In the place where it had been, there was just smooth skin, with a tiny little silver line.

Vision sat up. He touched his forehead. He put his hand on his chest. He felt the new heart beating.

"I'm still here," he whispered.

"You're still here," said Sydney, crying.

Wanda threw her arms around him.

Shuri held up the yellow stone. It glowed in her hand. "Now," she said. "Now we can destroy it. And he can never have it."

Wanda let go of Vision. She looked at the stone. She held out her hands. Red magic swirled around her fingers.

"Do it," said Vision gently. "I'll be fine. I promise. I've got a new heart."

Wanda pointed her hands at the stone. Red magic poured out of them. The yellow stone glowed. It shook. It cracked. And then it burst, in a huge flash of yellow and red light, into a thousand tiny sparkles that floated in the air like dust in a sunbeam.

"It's gone!" said Sydney. "It's gone! He can't have it!"

And then the wall of the lab broke open.

Thanos walked through the hole. He looked around at the sparkles floating in the air. He looked at Vision, sitting up on the table, alive. He looked at the yellow dust.

He didn't look angry.

He held up his glove. The green stone glowed.

And everything in the room went backward.

The sparkles floated back together. The dust swirled up into a tiny cloud. The cloud squeezed together. The cracks closed. And the yellow stone was whole again, floating in the air, right in front of Thanos.

"The Time Stone," whispered Shuri. "He turned back time."

Thanos reached out and took the yellow stone. He pushed it into his glove. Click.

Five stones. Blue. Purple. Red. Green. Yellow.

Only one empty hole left.

Wanda screamed and threw her red magic at him. Thanos brushed it away like a fly.

He looked down at Vision. He looked at the silver heart, glowing in Vision's chest.

"Clever," said Thanos. "Very clever."

Then he looked at Sydney. Standing next to Vision, in Shuri's too-big gloves, with her notebook sticking out of her pocket.

"You did this," said Thanos.

Sydney's knees were shaking. But she lifted her chin.

"Me and Shuri," said Sydney. "Kindness. It's the most important part."

Thanos looked at her for a long time.

Then he turned around and walked out of the lab, back onto the battlefield.

"Five stones," Thanos said, to everyone. To all of the heroes, standing there tired and hurt. "One left. And I know where it is. And I know what it costs." He looked across the field and found Gamora, standing next to Quill and Nebula. "I will come back for my daughter."

Gamora didn't flinch. But Quill stepped in front of her. And so did Nebula. And so did Lindsey, and Tim, and the three girls.

That's when Thor came out of the sky like a falling star.

CRAAAAACK!

He threw Stormbreaker with all his might. It flew through the air, spinning, glowing, crackling with lightning. Thanos turned and raised his glove.

Stormbreaker hit the golden glove. It smashed into it. Sparks exploded everywhere. Thanos was knocked off his feet. He flew backward across the field and landed on his back with a THUD that shook the whole valley.

For the first time, Thanos looked hurt.

He sat up slowly. He looked at his glove. One of the gold fingers was cracked.

Thor landed in front of him. Stormbreaker flew back into his hand. Lightning crackled everywhere.

"That," said Thor, "was for Asgard."

Thanos looked at him. Then he raised his cracked golden glove. The blue stone flickered. A portal opened behind him.

And Thanos stepped backward through it, and vanished.

The portal closed.

For a long time, nobody on the field said anything.

The outriders were gone. The Black Order was gone. The sun was going down over Wakanda, orange and pink and gold.

Everyone was tired. Everyone was scratched. Everyone was dusty. But everyone was there.

Steve walked across the field, slowly. Bucky walked beside him, helping Sam. Natasha walked with Rhodey. T'Challa and Okoye and M'Baku came. The Guardians came. Tony came, with Peter hanging onto his shoulder. Strange came, with his cape wrapped around Paisley.

They all found each other in the middle of the field.

Tim counted. He counted twice, to make sure. Then he counted a third time.

"Everybody's here," he said, and his voice cracked. "Every single person."

Lindsey pulled out her map with shaking hands. She turned it over. She looked at the list.

1. Space Stone. THANOS HAS IT.
2. Reality Stone. THANOS HAS IT.
3. Power Stone. THANOS HAS IT.
4. Mind Stone. She crossed out "In Vision's forehead" and wrote: THANOS HAS IT. (But Vision is okay!!!)
5. Time Stone. THANOS HAS IT.
6. Soul Stone (orange). On a planet called Vormir. THANOS CAN'T GET IT. Because we have Gamora.

Five circles. One without.

"He has five," said Lindsey quietly. "He only needs one more."

Everyone looked at Gamora.

"He can't have it," said Quill fiercely. "He can't have her."

"He won't," said Tim.

Just then, a jet landed at the edge of the field. The door opened. Out stepped a woman with a dark ponytail. Maria Hill. And behind her, in a long black coat with a patch over one eye, came Nick Fury.

He walked across the field. He looked at all the tired heroes. He looked at the family. He looked at the broken lab, and Vision sitting on the steps with his new silver heart, and Wanda holding his hand.

"I came as fast as I could," said Fury. "Looks like you didn't need me."

"We needed you," said Steve. "We just didn't have time to wait."

Fury nodded slowly. "Tell me everything."

So they did.

When they were done, Fury was quiet for a long time. Then he reached into his coat and pulled out a small, old, beat-up device. It was black and gray, with a tiny screen and a few buttons. It looked like something from a very long time ago.

"What's that?" asked Sydney.

"It's a pager," said Fury.

"What's a pager?" asked Lainey.

Tony groaned. "Oh boy. Here we go."

"It's like a phone," said Fury. "But older. Way older. And it only calls one person." He looked at the pager in his hand. "I've been carrying this for over twenty years. Waiting for a day when things got bad enough."

"Who does it call?" asked Paisley.

Fury looked up at the sky. The first stars were coming out.

"A friend," he said. "An old friend. Someone who is very, very far away. Someone who might be the strongest person I've ever met."

He looked at Tim.

"I think," said Fury slowly, "that today is the day."

He held out the pager.

"Would you like to push the button?"

Tim looked at the pager. He looked at Lindsey. He looked at his three girls, dusty and tired and brave.

"Let's all push it," said Tim. "Together."

So they did. Tim put his finger on the button. Lindsey put her finger on top of his. Then Lainey. Then Sydney. Then Paisley, right on top.

"One," said Paisley. "Two. Three."

They pushed.

Beep.

On the tiny screen, a little symbol appeared. A red and blue circle with a gold star in the middle.

And very, very far away, on the other side of the galaxy, someone's pager started to beep.

## Chapter 18: Captain Marvel Answers

It was Sunday.

Even with everything that was happening, it was still Sunday. And on Sunday, the family made spaghetti.

"We're going to need a bigger pot," said Lindsey.

They were in the giant kitchen at the Avengers Compound. Lindsey had found the biggest pot in the whole building. It was so big that Lainey could have taken a bath in it. And it still wasn't big enough.

Because this Sunday, everybody was coming.

Steve and Bucky and Sam. Natasha and Rhodey. Tony and Peter. Bruce. Thor. Wanda and Vision. Doctor Strange and Wong. Quill and Gamora and Drax and Mantis and Rocket and Groot. Nebula. T'Challa and Shuri and Okoye. Nick Fury and Maria Hill.

Tim rolled meatballs. Hundreds of them. Steve and Bucky helped. Bucky was very good at it, because his new vibranium arm never got tired.

Sydney and Shuri made garlic bread. Shuri invented a machine to butter it faster. It buttered the bread. It also buttered the counter, the wall, and Rocket.

"HEY!" said Rocket.

"Sorry!" said Shuri. "Version two will be better."

Lainey set the table. It was a very, very long table. She ran up one side and down the other with a stack of plates, putting them down as she went. Clink, clink, clink, clink, clink.

Paisley folded sparkly napkins. One for every chair. She folded one for Groot in the shape of a tree.

"I am Groot," said Groot, holding it up.

"You're welcome," said Paisley.

When everything was ready, everybody sat down at the long, long table. Steve said grace. Tim served the spaghetti. And for a little while, nobody talked about Thanos at all.

Thor ate seven plates. Then he held up his empty plate.

Everybody watched him. Even the Guardians, who didn't know the story, could tell something was about to happen.

Thor cleared his throat. "May I please have another?"

Lindsey smiled. "You may."

The whole table cheered. Thor stood up and bowed.

Drax looked confused. "Why is everyone cheering because he said please?"

"It's a long story," said Tim.

Then, outside the big windows, the sky lit up.

Not lightning. Not a rainbow. Something else. A bright golden glow, like a second sun, coming down out of the clouds. It got bigger. And brighter. And BRIGHTER. Until everyone at the table had to cover their eyes.

Then it landed on the lawn. Softly. Like a feather.

Everyone ran outside.

Standing in the middle of the grass was a woman. She had short blond hair and a red and blue suit with a gold star on the chest, exactly like the symbol on Fury's pager. Little sparks of golden light still flickered around her hands and her eyes. And in her arms, she was holding a big orange cat.

She looked around at all the people staring at her. She looked at the Compound. She looked at Fury.

"Okay," she said. "Where is he?"

Fury walked forward. "Hello, Carol."

"Nick," said the woman, smiling. Then she frowned. "You look old."

"It's been twenty years," said Fury.

"You still look old."

Fury almost smiled.

"Everyone," said Fury, "this is Carol Danvers. A long time ago, she got some very special powers. Now she flies around the galaxy, helping people on other planets. She's probably the strongest person in the universe." He paused. "Don't tell Thor I said that."

"I heard you," said Thor.

Carol looked at Thor. Thor looked at Carol. They looked each other up and down.

"I like this one," said Thor.

Then Paisley walked up. She wasn't looking at Carol. She was looking at the big orange cat.

"Is that your kitty?" asked Paisley.

"Sort of," said Carol. "His name is Goose."

"Can I pet him?" asked Paisley.

Fury held up his hand fast. "Careful. Goose is not exactly a cat."

"He looks like a cat," said Paisley.

"He's a Flerken," said Carol. "They look like cats. But they're not cats."

Paisley reached out and scratched Goose under the chin. Goose closed his eyes and purred. A big, rumbly, happy purr.

"He likes you," said Carol, surprised. "He doesn't like anybody."

Back inside, everybody sat down again to finish dinner. Carol sat next to Fury. Goose sat on Paisley's lap.

Then Goose saw a meatball on Tim's plate.

Goose's mouth opened. And opened. And OPENED. Wider than any cat's mouth should open. Out of it came four long, wiggly, pink tentacles, like an octopus. They grabbed the meatball off Tim's plate, pulled it into Goose's mouth, and then Goose closed his mouth, licked his whiskers, and went back to purring like nothing had happened.

The entire table stared.

"Okay," said Tim slowly. "So that happened."

"That," said Paisley, "is the BEST kitty in the whole universe."

Lainey looked at Fury. Then she looked at Goose. Then she looked at the patch over Fury's eye.

"Mr. Fury," she said. "How did you lose your eye?"

Fury sighed a very long sigh.

He pointed at Goose.

Everyone gasped.

"THE CAT DID THAT?" yelled Peter.

"Not a cat," said Fury and Carol at the exact same time.

"How did you two meet?" asked Sydney.

Carol leaned back in her chair and grinned. "A long time ago. In the nineteen-nineties. I crashed through the roof of a video store."

"What's a video store?" asked Lainey.

Tony groaned again. "Oh, I'm so old."

"It was a store where you rented movies," said Carol. "On big plastic tapes. You had to rewind them before you brought them back, or you got in trouble."

The girls looked at each other. This was the strangest thing they had heard all week, and they had met a raccoon who flew spaceships.

"Nick was a young agent back then," said Carol. "He came to see what fell through the roof. He had two eyes. And he had hair."

"HAIR?" said all three girls at once.

They turned and stared at Fury's shiny bald head.

"Everybody had hair in the nineties," said Fury.

"He also sang," said Carol. "In the car. Very loudly."

"I did not," said Fury.

"He did," said Carol, leaning toward Paisley. "Badly."

Paisley giggled so hard that Goose fell off her lap. Goose landed on his feet, gave everyone a very offended look, and climbed back up.

Then Carol's face got more serious. She looked at Fury.

"You never pushed the button," she said. "Not once in twenty years. I always wondered what it would take."

"I almost did," said Fury. "A couple of times." He looked down the long table at the family. "But somebody always showed up and fixed it first."

Carol followed his look. She studied Tim and Lindsey and the three girls for a long moment, the way a pilot studies the sky.

"Huh," she said. "Then I guess I'd better make myself useful."

After dinner, while everyone was cleaning up, Bruce came to find Paisley.

He looked nervous. He was wringing his hands.

"Paisley," he said. "Can I talk to you? Somewhere quiet?"

They went into a room at the end of the hall. It was empty, with a big window and a soft rug. Bruce sat down on the floor. Paisley sat down across from him.

"The Hulk still won't come out," said Bruce. "Not all the way. I can feel him in there. But he's scared. And I'm scared too. I've been scared of him my whole life. And he's been mad at me for being scared of him." He shook his head. "We're not very good at being friends."

Paisley thought about this. She tapped her crown.

"Remember what you said to me in the lab?" she said. "The very first time we met? You said the Hulk wasn't a bad guy. He just had really big feelings. And he didn't know what to do with them."

"I remember," said Bruce.

"Maybe," said Paisley, "you both have big feelings. And you both don't know what to do with them. So you should do them together."

Bruce looked at her.

"Breathe with me," said Paisley. "Both of you. In like a flower."

Bruce closed his eyes. He breathed in.

"Out like birthday candles."

He breathed out.

"In," said Paisley.

"Out," said Paisley.

This time, Bruce didn't fight it. He didn't try to push the Hulk away. He didn't try to pull him out. He just breathed. And he let the Hulk breathe with him.

His skin turned green. Slowly. Gently. Not all at once, like it usually did. Like a sunrise. He got bigger. And bigger. His shirt stretched. His shoulders grew. His hands grew.

But his eyes stayed the same. Kind. Calm. Brown. And his glasses stayed on his nose.

When it was done, a giant green man was sitting on the rug in front of Paisley. He was as big as the Hulk. As green as the Hulk. But his face was Bruce's face. And he was wearing a very, very stretched-out sweater.

He looked down at his big green hands. He looked at Paisley. He blinked.

"Huh," he said, in a voice that was deep like the Hulk's but smart like Bruce's. "Would you look at that."

Paisley's mouth fell open. "Bruce? Hulk?"

"Both," said the big green man. He smiled a huge smile. "Both of us. At the same time. We finally became friends. Me and me." He laughed, a big booming happy laugh. "You were right, Crown Girl. Big feelings really are easier with a friend."

Paisley jumped up and hugged him around the neck. He was so big that her arms didn't even reach halfway around.

He hugged her back. Very, very gently. Like holding a soap bubble.

When they walked back into the kitchen, everybody turned and stared.

"Hey, guys," said the big green Bruce, waving. "It's me. Us. It's a long story."

Tony dropped a plate. It broke. "Okay," he said. "Okay. Somebody's gotta explain this one."

"Paisley did it," said Bruce.

"Of course Paisley did it," said Tony.

Before the meeting, Lindsey found Gamora and Nebula sitting on the back steps of the Compound.

They were sitting very close together. But they weren't talking. Nebula was holding a piece of paper and a pencil. She was trying to draw something. She had already crumpled up four other pieces of paper. They were lying in a little pile at her feet.

"What are you drawing?" asked Lindsey, sitting down beside them.

"A map," said Nebula, without looking up. "Of the garden planet. Where he'll be." She frowned at the paper. "It's bad. I've never drawn a map before. Nobody ever taught me how to draw anything. Only how to fight."

Lindsey looked at the paper. There was a squiggly circle and some lines and a little square.

"It's not bad," said Lindsey. "It's a beginning. Every map starts as a beginning." She pointed. "What's the square?"

"His hut," said Nebula. "Where he sleeps."

"And the lines?"

"The fields. Tall yellow grass. He used to tell us about it. When we were little. Before bed." Nebula's voice got very quiet. "It was the only nice story he ever told."

Gamora put her hand on her sister's shoulder. Nebula looked surprised. But she didn't move away.

"I remember that story," said Gamora softly. "He said there would be two suns. And a little garden. And no one would bother him ever again."

"Two suns," said Lindsey. She took out her own pen. "Where were they in the sky? Left or right?"

Nebula thought. "Left. Low. Like they were about to set."

Lindsey drew two little circles in the corner of Nebula's map. "Then the shadows would fall this way," she said, drawing a line. "And anyone hiding behind this hill would be in the shade. Where he couldn't see them."

Nebula stared at the paper. Then she stared at Lindsey.

"You can tell all that from two suns?" she said.

"I can tell all that from you," said Lindsey. "You remembered the suns. You remembered the hill. You remembered everything. You're a better mapmaker than you think."

Nebula looked down at her drawing for a long time. Then she picked up the pencil again. And very carefully, she drew a little fence around the garden. And a scarecrow in the middle of the field.

"He made a scarecrow," she said. "Out of his old armor. He told us that, too."

"See?" said Lindsey. "Beginnings turn into maps."

Gamora looked at her sister, drawing. Then she looked at Lindsey. She didn't say thank you. She didn't have to. Lindsey could see it on her face.

Then everyone gathered in the big meeting room for the most important meeting of all.

Lindsey laid her map on the table. Back side up. The list of stones, with five big circles and one without.

"Okay," said Steve. "Here's what we know."

"Thanos has five stones," said Lindsey. "He needs six. The last one is the Soul Stone. On a planet called Vormir."

"And to get the Soul Stone," said Gamora quietly, "you have to give up the thing you love most."

Everyone looked at her.

"He'll come back for me," said Gamora. "He told me so. On the battlefield. He'll take me to Vormir. And he'll give me up to get the stone."

"No, he won't," said Quill.

"No, he won't," said Nebula, right at the same time. Then she looked surprised that she had said it.

"Where is he now?" asked Carol. "I'll go find him myself. Right now."

"I know where he is," said Nebula.

Everyone turned to look at her.

"When we were little," said Nebula, "he told us about a place. A quiet planet. Far away from everything. Covered in gardens and fields. He said that when he got all the stones and finished his work, he would go there and rest." She looked at her hands. "He's hurt. Thor hurt him, and his glove is cracked. He's tired. He'll go there. To rest. To get ready for the last stone."

"Then we go there," said Thor, gripping Stormbreaker. "Now. And we finish it."

"It's not that easy," said Tony. "He's still wearing the glove. Five stones. Even tired and hurt, he's the most powerful thing in the universe. We can't just walk up and punch him."

"Then we take the glove," said Rhodey.

"He never takes it off," said Gamora. "Not even to sleep. And it's welded onto his hand. Nobody could pull it off. We tried, remember?"

"So we can't take the glove off," said Sam. "And we can't fight him while he's wearing it. So what do we do?"

The room got very quiet.

Sydney was sitting on her knees on her chair, flipping through her notebook. Back, back, back, through Wakanda, through Spider-Man, through the Magic Doctor, back to a page with a drawing of a tiny man standing on a page of a notebook.

She stopped. She looked at it. She looked at the words underneath, with a star next to them.

ANT-MAN CAN GET INSIDE ANYTHING. EVEN A SUIT OF ARMOR.

"We don't take the glove," said Sydney slowly. "We take the stones out of the glove."

Everybody looked at her.

"The stones are like screws," said Sydney. She held up her hand and pointed at her knuckles. "They sit in little holes. Like the screws in Tony's boot. If somebody very, very tiny got inside the glove, they could loosen them. One by one. Twist, twist, twist. And the stones would just pop out."

"Who could get that tiny?" asked T'Challa.

Lindsey didn't even answer. She just flipped her map over to the front side. And under all the other notes, she pointed to the one that said:

ANT-MAN AND THE WASP. (Remember: they can sneak into ANYTHING.)

Tony looked at the map. He looked at Sydney. He started to grin.

"Scott Lang," said Tony. "Get me Scott Lang."

"Already did," said Lindsey. "I called him yesterday. Just in case."

At that exact moment, the front doorbell rang.

DING-DONG.

Everyone ran to the front door. Standing on the doorstep was Scott Lang, holding a backpack and a helmet. Next to him was Hope, in her black and silver Wasp suit, with her wings folded on her back.

And standing between them, holding her dad's hand, was a girl with brown hair and a gap in her front teeth.

"Hi," said Scott. "Lindsey called. Said you guys might need something small." He looked at the crowd of superheroes all staring at him. "Wow. That's a lot of Avengers. Hi, Captain America. Big fan. Huge fan." He held up his daughter's hand. "This is Cassie. Her mom's out of town. I hope it's okay I brought her."

"CASSIE!" yelled Paisley, and ran to hug her.

Cassie hugged her back. "Paisley! Do you still have Sparkle?"

"She's in my backpack!" said Paisley. "I brought her! Just in case!"

Everyone went back into the meeting room. Scott listened to the whole plan with his mouth hanging open.

"So you want me," said Scott slowly, "to shrink down really small. Fly into Thanos's giant golden glove. While he's wearing it. While he's sleeping. And take the Infinity Stones out. Like screws."

"Yes," said everyone.

Scott thought about it.

"Okay," he said. "That's the craziest heist I've ever heard of." He grinned. "I'm in."

"It's not crazy if there's a good map," said Lindsey.

She was already drawing it. Nebula told her what Thanos's garden planet looked like. Fields of yellow grass. A little hut made of stone. A garden of vegetables. A scarecrow made out of Thanos's old armor.

Lindsey drew it all. She drew the hut and the garden and the field. She drew where the ship would land, hidden behind a hill. She drew a path through the tall grass. She drew a big X on the hut.

Then she drew lots of little arrows, showing where everybody would go.

"It's beautiful," said Hope, looking over her shoulder.

"It's a heist map," said Lindsey. "Every heist needs a map."

"But what about the Soul Stone?" asked Natasha.

Everyone turned to look at her. She had been very quiet during the whole meeting.

"Even if we take five stones away from him," said Natasha, "the sixth one is still out there. On Vormir. Waiting. As long as it's out there, he'll always want it. And someday, he'll find a way to get it."

"So we go get it first," said Clint.

Clint had come in late. He had been at his farm with his wife, Laura, and his three kids. When Natasha called him, he had kissed them all goodbye and come right away. His bow was slung over his shoulder. The girls noticed tiny tooth marks on the handle.

"Baby," said Clint, when he saw them looking. "He's teething. It's a lucky bow now."

His phone buzzed on the table. A video call. A girl with a long brown ponytail popped up on the screen. She looked a little older than Lainey.

"Dad," she said. "You forgot your lunch."

"Lila, I'm kind of in a meeting," said Clint. "With the Avengers."

"Hi, Avengers," said Lila, waving. "He always forgets his lunch."

Everyone laughed. Even Steve.

Lila saw Lainey peeking over Clint's shoulder. "Are you the fast one?" she asked. "Dad told me about the fast one."

"I'm the fast one," said Lainey proudly.

"I'm the archer," said Lila. "When you come to the farm, I'll teach you to shoot. You teach me to run."

"Deal," said Lainey.

"Okay, okay," said Clint, laughing. "Love you, Lila. Tell your mom I'll be home soon."

"Promise?" said Lila.

Clint glanced at Natasha. Then at Tim. Then back at the phone.

"Promise," he said.

"If we get the Soul Stone first," said Clint, "he can never have it. Ever."

"But the price," said Gamora. "Someone has to give up the thing they love most. That's the rule. That's always the rule."

"Then we'll figure out a different way," said Natasha. "There's always another way. Sometimes you just have to look harder." She looked at Lindsey. "Somebody I know says that a lot."

Lindsey smiled.

"So we need two teams," said Steve. "One team goes to the garden planet. Sneaks up on Thanos. Takes the five stones out of his glove. And one team goes to Vormir. And gets the Soul Stone before he can."

"I'll go to Vormir," said Natasha.

"Me too," said Clint.

The room was quiet.

Then Tim stood up.

"I'll go with them," said Tim.

Everybody turned to look at him. Lindsey turned to look at him most of all.

"Tim," she said quietly.

"Somebody has to make sure everybody comes home," said Tim. "That's what a captain does. That's what Steve taught me. Nobody gets left behind." He looked at Natasha and Clint. "I can't fight like you. I can't shoot like you. But I can make sure you both come back. I promise."

Natasha looked at him for a long time. Then she nodded. "Okay, Captain," she said, and she didn't sound like she was joking.

Lindsey stood up too. She walked around the table. She stood in front of Tim. She didn't say anything for a long moment.

Then she reached into her pocket and pulled out a small, clean piece of paper. She smoothed it out on the table. She took out her pen. And while Gamora told her everything she remembered about Vormir, Lindsey drew.

She drew a cold, dark planet. A tall mountain. A long, twisty path up to the top. Two big stone pillars at the very peak.

When she was done, she folded the little map in half and put it in Tim's shirt pocket, right behind his star pin, right over his heart.

"So you can find your way back to me," said Lindsey.

Tim put his hand over the pocket. "Always," he said.

That night, nobody could sleep.
