The family went up to the roof of the Compound. It was cool and quiet. The stars were out. The lawn below was full of spaceships. The Milano. A big blue ship that Nebula said used to belong to Thanos, which they had borrowed. Carol's little ship. Tony's jet.

The five of them sat on a blanket in a row. Tim, Lindsey, Lainey, Sydney, Paisley.

Lindsey was holding her big map, the one with all the notes on the front and all the stones on the back. She kept unfolding it and folding it. Unfolding it and folding it.

"Mom," said Paisley. "You're doing the thing."

"What thing?" asked Lindsey.

"The thing where you keep folding and unfolding your map," said Paisley. "You only do that when you're really worried."

Lindsey looked down at her hands. Paisley was right.

"I'm worried about Dad," said Lindsey quietly. "Going to Vormir. Without me."

Paisley climbed into her lap. She took Lindsey's face in her two small hands.

"Mom," she said. "Breathe with me. In, like smelling a flower."

Lindsey looked at her. Then she laughed a little. And she breathed in.

"Out, like birthday candles."

Lindsey breathed out.

"In."

"Out."

"Now hug a puppy," said Paisley.

"We don't have a puppy up here," said Lindsey.

"You have me," said Paisley. And she hugged her.

Lindsey hugged her back. Very tight. For a long time.

"Who taught you that?" whispered Lindsey.

"You did," said Paisley.

Lainey and Sydney scooted over and joined the hug. Then Tim put his big arms around all of them.

For a while, nobody said anything. They just looked up at the stars.

"See that bright one?" said Lindsey softly, pointing. "That's the North Star. Remember? If you ever get lost at night, you can find it. And it will help you find your way home."

"Even on Vormir?" asked Sydney.

Lindsey thought about it. "Even on Vormir," she said. "Stars are stars. Everywhere."

Tim looked up at the North Star for a long time. He memorized it.

"Okay," he said. "I'll look for it."

Down on the lawn, the ships were getting ready. Carol was checking the engines with Rocket. Scott was doing stretches. Hope was checking her wings. Thor was telling Groot a very long story about a goat. Groot was not listening.

"Tomorrow," said Tim, "is going to be a really big day."

"Biggest ever," said Lainey.

"Bigger than the Battle of New York?" asked Sydney.

"Bigger than everything," said Lindsey.

Paisley yawned. Her crown was crooked. Her eyes were heavy.

"Daddy?" she said.

"Yes, sweetheart?"

"Do you think we'll all come home?"

Tim looked at her. He looked at Lindsey. He looked at Lainey and Sydney. Then he looked down at the lawn, at all their friends getting ready. At Steve, holding his shield. At Natasha, checking her batons. At Bruce, huge and green and gentle, carrying a box of supplies for Rocket. At Tony, putting his hand on Peter's shoulder. At Thor, holding Mjolnir with its shiny gold scars.

"I think," said Tim, "that we have a very good team."

Paisley smiled and closed her eyes.

Down on the lawn, Natasha looked up at the roof. She saw the five of them, all wrapped up together in one big hug on a blanket under the stars.

She watched them for a long time.

Then she turned to Clint. "I think I know what to do," she said.

"About what?" asked Clint.

"About Vormir," said Natasha. "About the price." She looked back up at the roof. "I just figured it out."

But she didn't say anything else. Not yet.

## Chapter 19: The Soul Stone

Early the next morning, before the sun came up, everyone gathered on the lawn.

There were two ships. The big blue one that used to belong to Thanos would carry the heist team to the garden planet. Nebula would fly it. Carol would fly beside it, glowing, to keep it safe.

The little silver one would go to Vormir. Rocket had fixed it up. "It's small," he said. "But it's fast. And it's got heated seats. You're welcome."

Natasha and Clint climbed into the little ship first.

Tim stood on the grass with his family.

Lindsey straightened his collar. She touched the little folded map in his shirt pocket, behind his star pin.

"You have the map," she said.

"I have the map," said Tim.

"You have your star pin."

"I have my star pin."

"You call me," said Lindsey. "If you get lost. If you're scared. If you just want to say hi. You call."

"I'll call," said Tim.

Lainey hugged his legs. Sydney hugged his middle. Paisley climbed up into his arms and hugged his neck so hard that his face turned red.

"Come back," whispered Paisley.

"I'll come back," said Tim. "I promise. Nobody gets left behind. Right?"

"Right," said Paisley.

He kissed each of them on the top of the head. He kissed Lindsey. Then he climbed into the little silver ship.

The door closed. The engines hummed. And the little ship zoomed up into the morning sky, smaller and smaller, until it was just a tiny star.

Lindsey stood on the lawn and watched until it was gone.

Then she unfolded her map, the big one, with all the notes. She turned it over to the list of stones. And under the Soul Stone, she wrote:

Tim, Natasha, and Clint went to get it. Before Thanos can.

"Okay," she said to the girls. She folded the map. "We have a heist to plan."

The little ship flew through space for a long time.

Clint sat in the pilot's seat, flying. Natasha sat next to him, looking out at the stars. Tim sat in the back, with his knees pulled up, looking at the little map Lindsey had drawn.

"Your wife draws a nice map," said Clint, looking back at him.

"She's the best finder in the whole world," said Tim.

"I believe it," said Clint. "She found Bruce in a building with a hundred rooms. She found Gamora on a ship the size of a city. She found Thanos's hiding planet from a story Nebula heard when she was a kid." He shook his head. "That's some serious finding."

Natasha was quiet. She was looking out the window.

"Nat," said Clint. "You've been quiet since last night. What did you figure out?"

Natasha didn't answer right away.

"I'll tell you when we get there," she said. "I want to make sure I'm right."

Clint and Tim looked at each other. But they didn't push.

After a while, Clint started talking about his kids. He had three. Lila, who was the oldest, and liked to shoot arrows just like her dad. Cooper, who was in the middle, and liked baseball. And Nathaniel, the baby, who liked to chew on everything.

"He chewed on my bow last week," said Clint. "Left little tooth marks on the handle. Laura says I should get a new one. I said no way. That's my lucky bow now."

Tim laughed. "Paisley chewed on my car keys when she was a baby. Lainey chewed on my shoes. And Sydney took apart the remote control."

"How old was she?" asked Clint.

"Two," said Tim.

Clint whistled. "Two."

"She put it back together, too," said Tim. "Mostly. It still changes the channel when you turn up the volume."

They laughed. Even Natasha smiled.

Then she turned around in her seat and looked at Tim.

"Can I tell you something?" she said.

"Sure," said Tim.

"When I was little," said Natasha, "I grew up in a place that wasn't very nice. It wasn't a home. It was a school. A school that taught girls to be spies. To be fighters. To be really, really good at keeping secrets. It didn't teach us anything else. Not how to have friends. Not how to have a family." She looked down at her hands. "When I got older, I did a lot of things I'm not proud of. I've spent my whole life since then trying to make up for them."

Tim listened. He didn't say anything. He just listened.

"I always thought," said Natasha, "that I'd have to keep making up for it forever. Like there was a giant scale. And I'd have to keep putting good things on one side, forever, until it balanced out." She was quiet. "And then I met you guys."

"Us?" said Tim.

"You," said Natasha. "Your family. The first time I saw Paisley calm down the Hulk. The first time I saw Lindsey find something nobody else could find. The first time I saw Lainey run so fast she made a whole crowd of people safe. The first time I saw Sydney notice something everybody else missed." She smiled a little. "The first time I saw you trip over a rug and knock out a Dark Elf."

"Thor told you about that?" said Tim.

"Thor told everybody about that," said Natasha.

Clint laughed so hard he almost steered the ship into a comet.

"You guys never kept score," said Natasha. "You just helped. Every time. Because that's who you are. And I realized, maybe that's who I am too. Now." She looked at him. "Maybe I don't have to keep making up for the old me. Maybe I just get to be the new one."

Tim reached up and put his hand on her shoulder.

"Nat," he said. "You're family. You've been family since the first time you walked across that field and told us you were a spy."

Natasha looked at him for a long time. Her eyes were shiny.

"Thanks, Captain," she said quietly.

Back at the Compound, Lindsey was spreading her heist map across the big table in the meeting room.

All the heroes on the heist team gathered around. Steve, Tony, Thor, Bruce. Rhodey, Sam, Bucky. Quill, Gamora, Nebula, Drax, Mantis, Rocket, Groot. Scott and Hope. Carol. T'Challa and Okoye. Peter. Wanda and Vision. Strange and Wong.

And the girls. Lainey and Sydney and Paisley, standing on chairs so they could see.

"Okay," said Lindsey. "Here's the plan. Every single person has a job."

She pointed at the map with her pen.

"The ship lands here. Behind the hill. Thanos won't be able to see it." She moved her pen. "Everyone walks through the tall grass on this path. Quietly. Very, very quietly. Mantis, you go first. If Thanos is awake, you help him get sleepy."

Mantis nodded seriously. Her antennas bobbed.

"When we get to the hut, Sydney checks his heartbeat." Lindsey pointed at the kimoyo bracelet on Sydney's wrist. "Shuri made a bead that can hear heartbeats from far away. If his heart is slow, he's sleeping. If it's fast, he's waking up."

Sydney held up her wrist. "Slow means go. Fast means stop."

"Then Scott and Hope shrink down," said Lindsey. "They fly into the glove through the crack Thor made with Stormbreaker. Scott loosens the stones. Hope catches them when they pop out. And Hope gives them to Lainey."

Lainey stood up very straight on her chair.

"Lainey carries the stones back to the ship," said Lindsey. "One at a time. As fast as she can. Because if Thanos wakes up, we need those stones far away from him. Fast."

"Faster than fast," said Lainey.

"Everyone else stays hidden around the hut," said Lindsey. "Ready. In case. If he wakes up, you protect the kids and get them back to the ship."

"And Paisley?" asked Steve.

Paisley stood up. "If he starts waking up," she said, "I sing him a lullaby. Mom always sings us lullabies when we can't sleep. It works every time."

The whole room was quiet.

"A lullaby," said Tony. "For Thanos."

"Even big guys need lullabies," said Paisley.

Bruce, big and green, nodded seriously. "She's not wrong," he said.

Thor laughed his big thunder laugh. "I would like to see that very much."

After the meeting, the girls sat on the floor of the big living room with a box of colorful string. Shuri had found it for them in her lab. Some of the string was regular. Some of it glowed in the dark. One spool was made of thin threads of vibranium, silver and shiny.

"What are you making?" asked Wanda, sitting down beside them.

"Bracelets," said Sydney. "For Dad and Natasha and Clint. For when they come home."

"So they know we were thinking about them the whole time," said Paisley.

Lainey was trying to tie a knot. It kept coming undone. She tried again. It came undone again. "Why won't it STAY?" she groaned.

Wanda smiled. She held up one finger. A tiny wisp of red magic floated out of it, soft as smoke. It wrapped around Lainey's string and tied it into a perfect little knot.

"Whoa," said Lainey. "Can you do the rest?"

"I'll do the hard knots," said Wanda. "You do the colors. That's the important part."

So they picked colors. Red and gold for Tim, like his star pin. Black and red for Natasha, like her spy suit. Purple for Clint, because Lainey said he looked like a purple kind of person.

Then Sydney took the silver vibranium thread and wove one strand through each bracelet. Right down the middle.

"That one's so they're strong," she said. "Vibranium can't break."

Paisley took a little marker. On a tiny flat bead on each bracelet, she wrote four letters: N.G.L.B.

"What does that mean?" asked Wanda.

"Nobody Gets Left Behind," said Paisley. "But it was too long to fit."

Wanda looked at the three little bracelets lying on the carpet. Her eyes were shiny.

"They're perfect," she said.

The girls put the bracelets in a little box. Then they put the box on the windowsill, right where they could see it. Where they could see the sky.

And they waited.

The little silver ship landed on Vormir.

It was a strange, dark planet. The sky was purple and black, with two moons glowing in it, one big and one small. The ground was covered in cold, crunchy snow. A sharp wind blew. And in front of them, rising up so high they couldn't see the top, was a mountain.

"Up there," said Natasha, pointing. "That's where it is."

They started to climb.

The path was narrow. It went back and forth, up and up, in a long zigzag. The wind blew harder the higher they went. Snow swirled. Their breath made little clouds.

"Watch your step," said Clint. "It's slippery."

Tim slipped. Of course he did. His feet went out from under him on a patch of ice. He started to slide.

Natasha grabbed his arm. Fast. Like lightning. She pulled him back up onto the path.

"Thanks," said Tim, breathing hard.

"You're welcome," said Natasha. "Try not to fall off a mountain on my watch."

"I'll do my best," said Tim.

They kept climbing. Higher and higher. The wind got stronger. The snow got thicker. And then, about halfway up, a big storm rolled in.

Snow blew sideways. The wind howled. They couldn't see the path anymore. They couldn't see the mountain. They couldn't see anything at all. Everything was white and gray and swirling.

"Which way?" shouted Clint. "I can't see!"

"I don't know!" shouted Natasha.

Tim pulled out Lindsey's little map. The wind almost ripped it out of his hand. He held on tight. But he couldn't tell where they were on it. Everything looked the same.

He pressed his star pin.

A tiny blue picture of Lindsey popped up in the snowy air. She was standing at the big table at the Compound, with her heist map. When she saw Tim, covered in snow, her eyes went wide.

"Tim! Are you okay?"

"We're okay," said Tim. "But there's a storm. A big one. We can't see the path. We don't know which way to go."

Lindsey didn't panic. Lindsey never panicked about maps.

"Okay," she said calmly. "Take out the map I drew. Find the two big stone pillars at the top."

Tim found them.

"Now," said Lindsey. "Gamora said the path always goes back and forth. Like a snake. Six turns left, six turns right, all the way to the top. How many turns have you taken?"

Tim looked at Natasha. Natasha looked at Clint.

"Seven," said Clint. "We just took the seventh turn. A right one."

"Then the next turn is left," said Lindsey. "Stay right next to the mountain. Keep your hand on the rock wall. Walk until the wall turns. Then turn with it."

Tim put his hand on the cold rock wall. Natasha held his other hand. Clint held hers. They walked. Step by step. Through the storm. Feeling the rock with their fingers.

Then the rock turned left. They turned left with it.

"Got it!" yelled Tim. "The turn! We found it!"

"Good," said Lindsey. "Keep going. Five more turns. I'm right here. I'm not going anywhere."

She stayed with them the whole way up. Every turn. Every step. She talked to them through the star pin, steady and calm, like a voice in the dark. Left. Right. Hand on the wall. You're almost there.

Behind her, at the Compound, the girls crowded around her, listening.

"Is Daddy okay?" whispered Paisley.

"Daddy's okay," said Lindsey. "Daddy's almost there."

Then, at the very top, the storm cleared. All at once. The wind stopped. The snow stopped. The clouds pulled back like a curtain.

And above them, in the purple sky, a star was shining. One bright star. Brighter than all the others.

Tim looked up at it. He smiled.

"I see it," he said. "The North Star. Or, I guess, the Vormir North Star."

"Stars are stars," said Lindsey softly. "Everywhere."

At the very top of the mountain, there were two tall stone pillars, just like Lindsey had drawn. Between them was a wide, flat place covered in snow. And on the other side of that, there was an edge. A long, dark drop down into nothing.

And floating above the snow, right between the pillars, was someone in a long, dark, ragged cloak.

They couldn't see a face. Just a hood, and darkness inside it. The cloak floated a few inches above the ground, moving slowly, like seaweed in the ocean.

"Welcome," said the floating figure. Its voice was raspy and tired, like an old door creaking. "Natasha Romanoff. Clint Barton. And... I do not know you."

"I'm Tim," said Tim.

"You are the Stonekeeper," said Natasha. "Aren't you?"

"I am," said the figure. "I guard the Soul Stone. I have guarded it for a very, very long time." It turned its hood toward them. "You have come for it. Everyone who comes here comes for it. And everyone who comes learns the same thing."

"That someone has to give up what they love most," said Clint.

"Yes," said the Stonekeeper. "The Soul Stone does not want power. It does not want gold. It does not want strength. It wants love. And it wants to know that you are willing to give it up. A soul for a soul." It pointed one long, thin finger toward the dark edge. "One of you must step into the dark. And stay there. Forever. Then the stone will be yours."

Everyone was quiet.

Natasha looked at Clint. Clint looked at Natasha.

"I'll do it," said Clint.

"No," said Natasha. "I will."

"Nat, I've got this," said Clint.

"Clint, you have Laura," said Natasha. "You have Lila and Cooper and Nathaniel. You have a lucky bow with tooth marks on it."

"And you have us," said Clint. "You have everybody. Every single one of the Avengers. You're the one who keeps us all together."

They stepped toward each other. Toward the edge.

"I'm going," said Natasha.

"No, I'm going," said Clint.

They grabbed each other's arms. Each of them trying to pull the other one back.

And then a third hand grabbed both of them.

Tim.

He stepped right in between them. He grabbed Natasha's arm with one hand and Clint's arm with the other. And he held on.

"No," said Tim. "Nobody's going. Nobody."

"Tim," said Natasha. "Let go. You don't understand. Somebody has to—"

"I understand," said Tim. "I understand perfectly. And the answer is no. Nobody gets left behind."

The Stonekeeper floated closer. "Then you will not get the stone."

"Then we won't get the stone," said Tim. His voice was shaking, but he didn't let go. "We'll go home without it. We'll find another way. There's always another way. You just have to look harder."

Natasha stared at him.

Then something changed in her face. She stopped pulling. She looked at Tim. She looked at Clint. And she started to laugh. A soft, surprised, happy laugh, with tears running down her cheeks.

"This is it," she said. "This is what I figured out."

"What?" said Clint.

Natasha turned to the Stonekeeper. She didn't let go of Tim's hand. She held it tighter.

"Last night," she said, "I watched a family sitting on a roof. Holding onto each other. All five of them. Wrapped up in one big blanket, looking at the stars. They were scared. They were worried. Tomorrow, one of them was going to a dark, cold planet far away. And not one of them let go."

She looked at the Stonekeeper.

"You said the Soul Stone wants love," said Natasha. "But love isn't something you give up. That's not how it works. Love is something you hold onto. Even when it's hard. Especially when it's hard." She held up her hand, holding Tim's, holding Clint's. "You want to know what we love most? This. Each other. And we're not going to give it up. Not for any stone. Not for anything."

Clint looked at her. Then he grabbed her other hand. The three of them stood in a little circle at the top of the mountain, holding on.

"You'd have to take all three of us," said Clint. "Or none of us."

The Stonekeeper didn't move. It didn't say anything. It just floated there.

The wind stopped. The snow stopped falling. Everything on the whole mountaintop went perfectly, perfectly still.

Then the Stonekeeper spoke. Very quietly.

"In all the years I have been here," it said, "every single person who came up this mountain came alone. Or they came with someone they were willing to give away. No one, in all that time, has ever come up this mountain and refused to let go."

It floated closer.

"The Soul Stone," said the Stonekeeper slowly, "was never asking you to give up love. It was asking whether you understood what love is." It tilted its hood. "You are the first ones who ever did."

Above them, the purple sky began to glow.

Orange light poured down. Warm, golden-orange light, like a sunrise, filling up the whole mountaintop. It wrapped around the three of them, around their hands, around their shoulders. It was warm. It felt like the biggest hug in the whole universe.

Tim felt something in his hand. Something small and smooth and warm.

He opened his fingers.

There, sitting in his palm, glowing softly orange, was a little stone.

The Soul Stone.

Natasha looked at it. Clint looked at it. Then they all looked at each other.

"We're all still here," said Clint, in a very small voice.

"We're all still here," said Natasha.

"Nobody got left behind," said Tim.

They hugged. All three of them at once. They hugged for a long, long time, standing at the top of the mountain, with the orange light slowly fading around them and the two moons shining down.

When they finally let go, the Stonekeeper was still floating between the pillars. But it looked different. Lighter, somehow. Less tired.

"What happens to you now?" asked Tim.

"Now," said the Stonekeeper, "for the first time in a very long time, I can rest." It turned toward the edge. Then it stopped. It turned back. "Tell the child with the crown," it said, "that someone was kind to me today. It has been a very long time."

"How did you know about Paisley?" asked Tim.

But the Stonekeeper was already gone. Floated away into the dark, like a puff of smoke. And the mountaintop was empty and quiet and peaceful, with nothing left but snow and starlight and the three of them.

Tim pressed his star pin with a shaking hand.

Lindsey's picture popped up instantly. She had been waiting. The girls were crowded around her, their faces all squished together in the tiny blue picture.

"Tim?" said Lindsey. "Tim, are you okay? What happened? Is everybody okay?"

Tim held up his hand. In the tiny blue picture, the girls could see what was in it.

A small, glowing, orange stone.

"We got it," said Tim. "And everybody's coming home. All three of us."

At the Compound, the whole room exploded.

The girls screamed and jumped up and down. Lindsey burst into tears. Steve whooped. Thor shouted so loud the windows rattled. Quill and Gamora hugged. Rocket fell off his chair. Groot said, "I AM GROOT!" so loudly that even people who didn't speak Groot knew exactly what he meant.

Bruce, big and green, picked up Paisley and Lainey and Sydney all at once and spun them around.

Lindsey wiped her eyes. She unfolded her big map. She turned it over to the list of stones. And under the Soul Stone, very carefully, with her hand shaking, she wrote:

WE HAVE IT. Tim and Natasha and Clint got it. Nobody gave anybody up. Nobody got left behind.

She drew a heart. Then she drew another heart. Then she drew a whole bunch of hearts, all the way around the edge of the paper.

On the mountain, Tim looked down at the Soul Stone in his hand.

"What do we do with it now?" asked Clint.

Natasha thought about it. "Thanos doesn't know we have it," she said. "He still thinks he has to come back for Gamora. He thinks he has to go to Vormir. He doesn't know we beat him to it."

"So we hide it," said Clint. "Somewhere safe. Where he'd never look."

Tim smiled. He reached into his shirt pocket. He took out Lindsey's little folded map. He wrapped the Soul Stone inside it, very carefully. Then he put it back in his pocket, right behind his star pin.

Right over his heart.

"There," said Tim. "Safest place I know."

They walked back down the mountain together. The storm was gone. The path was easy to see now. Two moons lit their way. And the bright, bright star shone above them the whole time.

At the bottom, Natasha stopped and looked back up at the mountain.

"You know," she said, "I came up here thinking I'd have to give something up."

"And?" said Clint.

"And instead," said Natasha, "I think I got something back."

She looked at Tim.

"Me," she said. "I got me back."

Tim put one arm around her and one arm around Clint, and the three of them walked to the little silver ship.

When they got back to the Compound, it was the middle of the night. But everybody was waiting on the lawn. Every single person.

The girls reached Tim first. Lainey, of course. Then Sydney. Then Paisley. They nearly knocked him over. Then Lindsey got there. And she didn't knock him over. She just held him, for a long, long time, without saying anything.

"I found my way back," Tim whispered.

"I know," whispered Lindsey. "I was watching the whole time."

Then Sydney ran inside and came back with the little box from the windowsill. She opened it. The girls handed out the bracelets.

Red and gold for Tim. Black and red for Natasha. Purple for Clint.

Clint looked at his. He turned it over and found the little bead. "N.G.L.B.?"

"Nobody Gets Left Behind," said all three girls at once.

Clint put it on right away. He held up his wrist and showed it to Natasha.

Natasha was holding hers very carefully, like it might break. She looked at it for a long time. Then she looked at the girls.

"Nobody's ever made me anything before," she said quietly.

"Well," said Paisley, "now somebody has."

Natasha put it on. She didn't take it off. Not that night. Not ever.

Then Paisley tugged on his sleeve.

"Daddy," she said. "Did you meet the Stonekeeper?"

"I did," said Tim. He knelt down in front of her. "And the Stonekeeper wanted me to tell you something."

"What?"

"It said, 'Tell the child with the crown that someone was kind to me today. It's been a very long time.'"

Paisley thought about that. She tapped her crown.

"But I wasn't there," she said. "I didn't even talk to it."

Tim smiled. He looked at Natasha, standing with Clint on the grass. He looked at Lindsey. He looked at all the people on the lawn.

"I think," said Tim, "you were there. A little bit. In all of us."

Paisley thought about that, too.

Then she smiled. And she reached into Tim's pocket, behind his star pin, very carefully. She touched the little folded map, with the warm, glowing stone wrapped up inside.

"Good," she said. "I'm glad it's not lonely anymore."

## Chapter 20: The Big Shrink Heist

The flight to the garden planet took all night.

The big blue ship was crowded and quiet. Nobody could sleep. Everybody was too nervous.

Scott sat in the corner, practicing. He shrank. ZOOP. He got big. ZOOP. He shrank again. He shrank so many times that Rocket finally threw a sock at him.

"You're making me dizzy," said Rocket.

"I'm making ME dizzy," said Scott. "But I have to get it right. One mistake and I'm stuck inside a giant purple guy's glove forever."

"You won't make a mistake," said Hope, sharpening a tiny sword that was smaller than a toothpick.

"How do you know?"

"Because I'll be right next to you," said Hope.

Scott stopped shrinking. He smiled at her.

Sydney sat at a little table with a model of the glove that Shuri had made for her out of plastic. It had five holes, each with four tiny screws around it. Sydney practiced with a tiny screwdriver. Twist, twist, twist. Lefty-loosey. Twist, twist, twist.

"Again," said Shuri, through her kimoyo bead. "Faster this time."

Twist, twist, twist. Twist, twist, twist.

"Perfect," said Shuri. "You could do it in your sleep."

"I might have to," yawned Sydney.

Lainey practiced too. Rocket had set up a little race course down the middle of the ship. Lainey ran from the back to the front, carrying a rubber ball, and dropped it into a box. Rocket timed her with a stopwatch.

"Four seconds," said Rocket. "Again."

She ran it again. "Three and a half."

She ran it again. "Three. Okay. Now do it carrying a glowing rock of infinite power while a giant purple guy chases you."

"Easy," said Lainey.

"That's the spirit," said Rocket.

And in the very back of the ship, Paisley practiced her lullaby.

She sang it to Bruce. Big, green Bruce, sitting cross-legged on the floor, because he was too big for any of the seats.

*Hush now, hush now, the stars are awake...*

By the second verse, Bruce's eyes were drooping. By the third verse, he was leaning against the wall. By the end of the song, he was snoring.

"HNNNRRRRK."

Paisley turned around and looked at everyone with a very serious face.

"It works," she said.

Thor laughed so loud that Bruce woke up with a snort. "What? What happened? Did I miss the heist?"

"You fell asleep," said Thor, wiping his eyes. "In one song. The strongest man I know. In one song."

"It's a really good song," said Bruce, a little embarrassed.

Lindsey walked up and down the ship with her heist map, quizzing everybody.

"Mantis. Where do you go first?"

"Front of the line. Make him sleepy."

"Sydney. Slow means?"

"Go."

"Fast means?"

"Stop."

"Tim. What's your job?"

"Count everybody," said Tim. "Every single person. Every single time. Nobody gets left behind."

"And Thor," said Lindsey. "What's your job?"

"Do not laugh," said Thor seriously. "No matter what happens. Do not laugh."

"Can you do that?"

Thor thought about it. "I will try very hard."

Lindsey folded up her map. She looked at all of them. All the heroes, nervous and tired and ready.

"Okay," she said. "Then let's go steal some stones."

The garden planet was the quietest place the family had ever seen.

It had two suns. One big and yellow, one small and orange. They hung low in the sky, making everything look golden, like the very end of a summer afternoon. Tall yellow grass swayed in a soft breeze. There were no birds. No cars. No sounds at all, except the wind and the grass.

The big blue ship landed behind a hill, exactly where Lindsey's map said it should. The engines went quiet. The door slid open.

Everybody crept out. Very, very quietly.

"Remember," whispered Lindsey, holding up the heist map. "Stay on the path. Stay low. Nobody talks unless they have to."

Everyone nodded.

Tim was the last one out. He had his hand pressed against his shirt pocket. Behind his star pin, wrapped inside Lindsey's little map, the Soul Stone was glowing softly. Nobody had wanted to leave it behind.

"Thanos doesn't know we have it," Natasha had said. "So the safest place for it is the last place he'd look. Right under his nose."

"In my pocket," Tim had said.

"In your pocket," Natasha had said.

They crept through the tall grass in a long line. Mantis went first, her antennas twitching. Then Steve. Then the girls, with Tim and Lindsey right behind them. Then everyone else.

Halfway across the field, Lainey suddenly froze. She grabbed Sydney's arm. She pointed.

Standing in the middle of the grass, very tall, with its arms stretched out, was Thanos.

Lainey opened her mouth to scream. Sydney slapped her hand over it.

Then Sydney looked closer.

It wasn't Thanos. It was just his armor. His big gold helmet and his gold chest plate, hung up on two crossed sticks in the middle of the field. Like a scarecrow.

"It's a scarecrow," whispered Sydney. "He made a scarecrow out of his armor."

Lainey let out her breath very slowly. "I knew that," she whispered.

"Sure you did," whispered Sydney.

Behind them, Thor was trying very hard not to laugh. His shoulders were shaking. Bruce put one big green hand gently over Thor's mouth.

At the end of the field, there was a little hut made of stones, with a thatched roof and a small garden of vegetables growing out front. There was a wooden porch. There was a big wooden chair on the porch, with a blanket on it.

And smoke was coming out of the chimney.

"He's inside," whispered Nebula. "Cooking. He always made soup at the end of the day."

They crept up to the side of the hut. They peeked through the window.

Thanos was sitting inside, in a big stone chair by the fire. He had taken off his armor. He was just wearing simple brown clothes, like a farmer. A pot of soup bubbled over the fire.

His eyes were closed. His big chin was resting on his chest.

And on his left hand was the golden glove.

Five stones glowed in it. Blue. Red. Purple. Green. Yellow. The glove was cracked and burned and dented where Stormbreaker had hit it. One golden finger was split almost all the way open. But it was still on his hand. And all five stones were still inside.

He was asleep.

And he was snoring.

"HHHHRRRRRRRNNNNNK. Shhhhhhhhhhh. HHHHRRRRRRRNNNNNK. Shhhhhhhhh."

It was the loudest snore anybody had ever heard.

Lainey leaned over to Tim and whispered, "That's even louder than you."

"I do NOT snore like that," whispered Tim.

"You kind of do," whispered Lindsey.

Sydney held up her wrist. She pressed the special kimoyo bead that Shuri had given her. A tiny line of light glowed on it. It went up and down, up and down, slowly. Bump. Bump. Bump.

"His heart is slow," whispered Sydney. "He's sleeping. Really sleeping."

"Slow means go," whispered Lindsey.

She looked at Scott. Scott looked at Hope. Hope nodded.

"Okay," whispered Scott. "Here we go. The craziest heist in the history of the universe."

He pressed the button on his glove. ZOOP. He shrank down to the size of an ant. Hope pressed hers. ZOOP. She shrank down too, her clear wings buzzing.

Then Paisley knelt down and opened her backpack. She took out the little glass ant farm. Sparkle was inside, cleaning her antennas.

"Sparkle," whispered Paisley. "You're the best squeezer in the whole universe. Go show Scott the way in."

She opened the lid. Sparkle climbed out onto the windowsill. Tiny Scott climbed onto Sparkle's back, like riding a horse. Tiny Hope flew beside them.

And the three of them slipped through a crack in the window and into the hut.

Everyone held their breath.

Inside the hut, everything was enormous. The floor was like a giant wooden desert. The fire was like a huge orange mountain. And Thanos was like a purple planet, breathing in and out, in and out, with a noise like a thunderstorm.

Sparkle ran across the floor. Up the leg of the stone chair. Along the arm of the chair. Right up to the giant golden glove.

Up close, the glove was like a golden cave. The cracks in it were like canyons. And deep inside, through the biggest crack in the split finger, Scott could see the stones. They were glowing like giant colored lanterns.

"Wow," whispered Scott into his helmet. "Okay. That's a lot of glowing."

"Focus," whispered Hope.

Sparkle squeezed into the crack in the golden finger. It was so tight that Scott had to lie flat on her back. But Sparkle was the best squeezer in the whole universe. She wiggled and wiggled and wiggled, and then they were through.

They were inside the glove.

The five stones sat in five round holes, each one held in place by four tiny golden clamps, like the prongs on a ring. And each clamp was held tight by a tiny, shiny screw.

"Sydney," whispered Scott into his headset. "You were right. They're screws. Little tiny golden screws."

Outside, Sydney grinned so big her cheeks hurt.

"The tiniest, shiniest ones," she whispered back. "Lefty-loosey. Righty-tighty. Turn them left."

Scott took out a tiny tool from his belt. He put it on the first screw, next to the blue stone. He turned it left.

Twist, twist, twist.

The screw came loose. He did the next one. Twist, twist, twist. And the next. And the next.

The blue stone wiggled in its hole.

Then it popped out, rolled down the inside of the glove, and slipped right out through the crack in the golden finger. It fell toward the floor.

Hope was there. She got big, ZOOP, just for a second, caught the blue stone in her hand, and handed it out the window to Lainey.

Lainey took it. It was warm. It hummed in her fingers.

She ran.

She ran through the tall grass, low and fast and silent, all the way back over the hill to the big blue ship. Rocket and Nebula were waiting at the door with a box. A special box made of vibranium, that Shuri had built. It had six round spaces inside, lined with soft purple cloth.

Lainey put the blue stone in the first space.

"One," said Rocket.

Lainey ran back.

"Got one," whispered Tim into his pin. "Lainey made it. One down."

Inside the glove, Scott was already working on the red stone. Twist, twist, twist. Twist, twist, twist. It popped out. Hope caught it. Lainey ran.

"Two," said Rocket.

Then something happened.

Thanos stopped snoring.

Everyone outside the hut froze.

Sydney looked at her bracelet. The little line of light was moving faster. Bump-bump. Bump-bump. Bump-bump.

"His heart's going faster," she whispered. "He's waking up!"

Fast means stop.

Inside the glove, Scott stopped. He held completely still. Sparkle held completely still.

Thanos shifted in his chair. He grumbled. His big hand twitched. His eyelids fluttered.

Mantis crept to the window. She reached through and touched his foot with one finger. Her antennas glowed. "Sleeeeep," she whispered.

Thanos sighed. But he didn't fall back asleep. His eyes were still fluttering.

"I can't hold him," whispered Mantis. "He's too strong. He's too close to waking."

Everyone looked at each other.

And then Paisley stood up.

She walked right up to the open window. She put her chin on the windowsill. She looked at Thanos, huge and purple, twitching in his big stone chair.

And she started to sing.

Very softly. Very gently. The same lullaby Lindsey sang to her every night when she couldn't sleep.

*Hush now, hush now, the stars are awake,*
*watching the meadow and watching the lake.*
*Close your eyes, little one, rest your head,*
*the moon is a blanket all over your bed.*

Thanos stopped twitching.

*Hush now, hush now, the wind's gone to sleep,*
*the hills and the rivers are quiet and deep.*
*Nobody's leaving, and nobody's lost,*
*we'll hold on together, whatever the cost.*

Thanos's big shoulders relaxed. His hand went still. His head slowly drooped back down onto his chest.

And then, very quietly, he started to snore again.

"HHHHRRRRRNNNNK. Shhhhhhhhh."

Sydney looked at her bracelet. Bump. Bump. Bump. Slow again.

Outside the hut, every single hero was staring at Paisley with their mouths hanging open.

Thor had tears running down his face. He wasn't even trying to hide them.

"That," whispered Tony, "was the most terrifying and adorable thing I have ever seen."

"Even big guys need lullabies," whispered Paisley.

Lindsey hugged her so tight that her crown went crooked.

Scott got back to work. Twist, twist, twist. The green stone. Pop. Hope caught it. Lainey ran.

"Three," said Rocket.

Twist, twist, twist. The yellow stone. Pop. Hope caught it. Lainey ran.

"Four," said Rocket.

Then Tim had a little accident.

He was crouching by the side of the hut, keeping count, holding the line of heroes together. He shifted his foot to get comfortable. And he stepped on a rake that had been left lying in the grass.

The handle of the rake swung up.

BONK. Right in the middle of his forehead.

Tim didn't make a sound. Not one sound. He just stood there, with his eyes squeezed shut, mouthing the word OW over and over.

Every single hero turned and looked at him.

Thor slapped both of his own hands over his mouth. His whole body shook. Bruce put one big green hand over Thor's hands, just to be extra sure. Rhodey bit his lip so hard it turned white. Sam had to turn around and face the other way. Quill was crying silent tears of laughter into Gamora's shoulder.

Even Natasha's mouth was twitching.

Inside the hut, Thanos snored on.

"Seriously?" whispered Lindsey, trying not to laugh.

"It was a rake," whispered Tim, rubbing his forehead. "Who leaves a rake in the grass? On a secret planet?"

"Thanos does, apparently," whispered Steve.

Only one stone was left. The purple one. The Power Stone.

Scott put his tiny tool on the first screw. He turned it left.

It didn't move.

He turned it harder. It still didn't move. He pushed with all his tiny strength. Nothing.

"It's stuck," whispered Scott. "This one's stuck. It won't turn."

"Try harder!" whispered Lainey.

"I'm trying!" whispered Scott. "It's not moving at all! It's like it's glued!"

Outside, Sydney closed her eyes and thought.

She thought about Tony's boot rocket, with the loose blue wire. She thought about the backwards wires on the Grandmaster's ship, red to blue and blue to green. She thought about the computer chip on the Helicarrier that wouldn't go in because it was upside down.

She opened her eyes.

"Scott," she whispered. "Try the other way."

"What?"

"Turn it RIGHT," whispered Sydney. "It's the Power Stone. It's the most dangerous one. If I were Thanos, I'd make that screw backwards. So nobody could steal it. Lefty-tighty. Righty-loosey. Like the Grandmaster's wires. Backwards."

Inside the glove, Scott put his tool on the screw. He turned it right.

Twist, twist, twist.

It came loose.

"YOU'RE A GENIUS!" whispered Scott, so loudly it almost wasn't a whisper.

He loosened the next one. Righty-loosey. And the next. And the last one.

The purple stone wiggled in its hole.

It popped out.

And just as it popped, it hummed. A deep, loud, buzzing HUMMMMMM, like a giant bumblebee. The whole hut shook. The soup pot rattled. The fire flickered.

Thanos's eyes snapped open.

He looked down at his glove. He saw five empty holes.

He saw a tiny man riding an ant, sliding down his golden finger. He saw a woman with clear wings catching a purple stone in her hand.

He saw a little girl in sneakers standing at the window.

"You," said Thanos.

Hope threw the purple stone out the window. Lainey caught it.

"RUN!" yelled everybody at once.

Lainey ran.

Thanos stood up. He was so big that his head went right through the thatched roof. Straw flew everywhere. He ripped the broken glove off his hand and threw it on the floor. He stepped out of the hut, right through the wall. CRASH. Stones went flying.

And he went after Lainey.

Lainey ran through the tall yellow grass. Faster than she had ever run. The purple stone was buzzing in her hand. Behind her, the ground shook. BOOM. BOOM. BOOM. Thanos was taking giant steps. Each one was as long as a whole bus.

He was getting closer.

"LAINEY!" screamed Lindsey.

"I'm okay!" yelled Lainey, still running. "I'm the fastest runner anyone's ever seen!"

Thanos reached for her with one giant purple hand.

And then a blur of red and gold and blue came out of the sky.

Carol Danvers hit Thanos like a meteor. WHAM! Golden light exploded everywhere. Thanos went flying backward through the grass.

Thor came down next, with Stormbreaker in one hand and Mjolnir in the other. CRACK! Lightning hit Thanos and knocked him down again.

Bruce came next. Big and green and smart. He grabbed Thanos by the arm and threw him into a hill. BOOM.

Steve came next, with his shield. Then everybody. Every single hero, standing in a line between Thanos and the little girl running through the grass.

Lainey reached the ship. Rocket grabbed the purple stone and dropped it in the vibranium box.

"FIVE!" yelled Rocket. He slammed the lid. CLICK. "Let's GO!"

"Everybody back to the ship!" yelled Tim. "Everybody! Go, go, go!"

The heroes ran. Scott and Hope zoomed back, Sparkle riding on Hope's shoulder. Paisley scooped up Sparkle and put her in the ant farm. Tim stood at the ship door, counting heads as everyone ran past.

"Steve. Bucky. Sam. Natasha. Thor. Bruce. Tony. Peter. Rhodey. Quill. Gamora. Drax. Mantis. Nebula. Groot. Scott. Hope. Carol. T'Challa. Okoye. Wanda. Vision. Strange. Wong." He looked at Lindsey and the girls, already on board. "Lindsey. Lainey. Sydney. Paisley." He took a breath. "Everybody. Everybody's here."

He jumped on. The door slammed shut. The ship roared up into the golden sky.

Through the window, they could see Thanos, standing up slowly in the middle of the yellow field. His farmer's clothes were dirty. His hut was broken. His glove lay empty on the ground.

He looked up at the ship.

He didn't yell. He didn't scream. He just watched them fly away.

Then, very quietly, he spoke. And somehow, even from all the way up in the sky, they could all hear him.

"I will come for them," said Thanos. "All six of them. On your little blue planet. And I will not come alone."

Then the ship flew into the clouds, and he was gone.

Back at the Compound, everybody gathered around the big table.

Rocket set down the vibranium box. He opened the lid.

Five stones glowed inside, sitting on soft purple cloth. Blue. Red. Purple. Green. Yellow.

And one empty space.

Tim reached into his shirt pocket. He took out Lindsey's little folded map. He unfolded it, very carefully. And there in the middle, glowing warm and orange, was the Soul Stone.

He placed it gently in the sixth space.

Six stones. All together. Glowing so bright that the whole room was full of colors. Like being inside a rainbow.

Nobody said anything for a long time.

"We did it," whispered Sydney. "We have all six."

"Thanos doesn't have any," said Lainey.

"We got them all back," said Paisley. "And nobody got hurt."

"Well," said Tim, rubbing his forehead. "Almost nobody."

Everybody laughed. Thor laughed so hard he fell out of his chair. Bruce laughed so hard the table shook. Even Nebula laughed, a little rusty laugh, like she was trying it out for the first time.

Cassie came running into the room. She had been waiting at the Compound with Maria Hill the whole time.

"Daddy! Did it work? Did Sparkle help?"

Scott picked her up. "Sparkle," he said, "was the hero of the whole heist. She found the way in. Without her, I'd still be standing outside that glove, knocking."

Paisley held up the ant farm. Sparkle was inside, running happy little circles in the sand.

Shuri leaned over and looked at Sparkle very closely. Then she pulled something out of her pocket. It was a tiny silver circle, smaller than a grain of rice, with a tiny purple dot in the middle.

"I made it while you were gone," said Shuri. "I was too nervous to sleep." "Out of a little scrap of vibranium. It's a medal."

She opened the ant farm. Very gently, with a pair of tweezers, she set the tiny medal down in the sand right in front of Sparkle.

Sparkle stopped. She looked at it. She waved her antennas. Then she picked it up in her tiny jaws and carried it down into her tunnel. Into the room that looked a little like a crown.

"She likes it," whispered Paisley.

"She earned it," said Shuri.

Then Lindsey unfolded her big map. She turned it over to the list. And next to every single stone, all six of them, she crossed out THANOS HAS IT. And she wrote, in big happy letters:

WE HAVE IT.

Then she flipped the map to the front. She looked at all the notes. LOKI = TROUBLE. FIND BUCKY. FIND BRUCE. THANOS = WANTS ALL SIX STONES.

Under THANOS, she wrote:

...but he doesn't have ANY.

"He's going to come," said Gamora quietly. "He said so. He's going to bring his whole army. His giant ship. Everything he has. He'll come here, to Earth, to take them back."

"Then we need a plan," said Steve.

Tony was staring at the six stones in the box. His eyes were very bright. He was thinking very, very fast.

"I have one," said Tony. "Or, the beginning of one. We build a new glove. Our own glove. And we use the stones. Just once. To send Thanos and his whole army away. Far, far away. To an empty corner of space where they can never come back."

"Like the Power Stone did to Ronan," said Sydney.

"Exactly like that," said Tony. "Except with all six."

Bruce frowned. "Tony. All six stones at once. That's a lot of power. A lot. Way more than the Power Stone by itself. Whoever wears that glove..." He shook his big green head. "I don't know if anyone could hold that much power. Not even me. It might be too much for anyone."

The room got quiet.

Then Sydney opened her notebook. She flipped back, back, back, past Wakanda, past Spider-Man, past Ant-Man, past the Magic Doctor, past Ultron, all the way back to a page from Xandar.

On it was a drawing of a long chain of people holding hands. With a big star around it. And words in big letters:

THE POWER STONE IS TOO STRONG FOR ONE PERSON.
BUT NOT FOR A LOT OF PEOPLE HOLDING HANDS.

She held it up.

Tony looked at it. Bruce looked at it. Quill and Gamora and Drax and Rocket looked at it, and remembered the purple light on Xandar. The chain of hands. The power spreading out, until it felt like a warm hug.

"It works," said Quill slowly. "We did it. On Xandar. It works."

"Then that's the plan," said Tony. "We build the glove. And when it's time to use it, nobody does it alone."

"Nobody gets left behind," said Tim.

Tony looked at him. He smiled.

"Nobody gets left behind," said Tony.

They worked all night. Tony and Bruce and Shuri and Rocket built the new glove in Tony's lab. It was made of red and gold metal, Tony's colors, with tiny robot pieces that moved like water. It had six round holes in it, one for each stone.

Sydney helped. She held the flashlight. She passed the tiny screws. The tiniest, shiniest ones.

"This one," she said, holding one up. "Righty-tighty."

"Righty-tighty," agreed Tony. He paused. "Hey, Syd."

"Yeah?"

"Remember the first day we met? When I said you put the rockets on the bottom of the feet, just like me?"

"I remember," said Sydney.

"I was wrong about one thing," said Tony. "You don't build things just like me." He smiled at her. "You build them better. You build them with kindness."

Sydney didn't say anything. She just handed him the next screw.

By the time the sun came up, the glove was done. It sat on a table in the middle of the lab, red and gold and shining, with six stones glowing in it. Blue. Red. Purple. Green. Yellow. Orange.

Everybody stood around it, tired and quiet.

Then, all at once, every alarm in the Compound went off.

WEE-OOO! WEE-OOO! WEE-OOO!

Every star pin went off at the same time. Not a beep. A siren.

Everybody ran to the windows.

The morning sky over the Compound was going dark. Not cloudy dark. Shadow dark. Something enormous was coming down out of the clouds. It was gray and black and spiky, like a giant metal flower.

The Sanctuary. Thanos's ship.

It was bigger than the whole Compound. Bigger than the whole field. Bigger than anything any of them had ever seen. It hung in the sky above them like a storm.

And out of the bottom of it, like water pouring out of a bucket, came the army.

Thousands and thousands of outriders. Flying ships. Giant creatures with armored backs. And walking down a ramp of light, right in the middle of it all, wearing his golden armor again, came Thanos.

He looked up at the Compound. He looked straight at the window where the family was standing.

Tim put his arm around Lindsey. Lindsey held the girls.

"Well," said Tim quietly. "He said he'd come."

## Chapter 21: The Final Battle

Thanos walked down the ramp of light and stepped onto the lawn.

His army was still pouring out of the giant ship behind him. Outriders, running on all their legs. Flying ships, swooping and diving. Huge creatures as big as buildings, with armored backs and long tails. They spread out across the far end of the field, more and more and more of them.

But Thanos didn't wait for them. He started walking. Straight toward the Compound. Straight toward the window where the family was standing.

Inside, everyone was running.

"Get the glove!" shouted Steve. "Get everybody together!"

Lindsey was already moving. She had studied every map of the Compound. She knew every hallway, every staircase, every door. And she knew something that almost nobody else knew.

"The tunnels!" she said. "There are tunnels under the building! Tony built them for emergencies! They go all the way out to the woods!"

Tim grabbed the vibranium box with the new glove inside. Lainey grabbed Sydney's hand. Sydney grabbed Paisley's.

But Paisley wasn't moving. She was standing at the window. And standing next to her, very big and very green, with his glasses on his nose, was Bruce.

He was watching Thanos walk across the lawn. His big hands were shaking.

"Bruce?" said Paisley.

"That's him," said Bruce quietly. "That's the one who beat me. In New Asgard. He beat the Hulk like it was nothing. And I was so scared afterward that the Hulk wouldn't come out for weeks." He took a breath. "Last time, I was scared."

"Are you scared now?" asked Paisley.

Bruce looked down at her. At her sparkly crown with the red-and-gold star pin. At her small hand, holding onto his giant green finger.

"No," said Bruce. "Not this time."

He knelt down so he was eye to eye with her.

"Everybody needs time to get to the tunnels," he said. "So I'm going to go say hi to our purple friend. And keep him busy."

Paisley's eyes went big. Then she remembered something. Something from a long time ago, on a street in New York, when a giant metal whale was swimming toward them.

"Remember what you said?" she said. "In the Battle of New York? You said today you were going to let your big feelings out on purpose. For something good."

Bruce smiled. "I remember."

"Then do it again," said Paisley. "For something good."

Bruce stood up. He cracked his big green knuckles. He took one deep breath. In, like a flower. Out, like birthday candles.

Then he jumped straight through the window.

CRASH!

Glass sprayed everywhere. Bruce landed on the lawn with a BOOM that shook the whole building. He landed right in Thanos's path.

Thanos stopped walking.

He looked at the big green man standing in front of him. He tilted his head.

"The green one," said Thanos. "You ran from me last time."

"I didn't run," said Bruce. "You threw me. It's different."

"You're smaller than before," said Thanos. "And you're wearing glasses."

"Yeah," said Bruce. "Me and the big guy made friends. Turns out we're a lot stronger together."

Thanos almost smiled. "We shall see."

They charged.

BOOM!

They crashed together in the middle of the lawn, like two mountains hitting each other. The ground cracked. Windows rattled. The family, running down the hallway inside, felt the floor shake under their feet.

Thanos swung his giant fist. Bruce blocked it. WHAM. Bruce swung back. Thanos blocked it. WHAM. Thanos grabbed Bruce and threw him into a tree. Bruce got up, ran back, grabbed Thanos by the armor, and threw him into the flagpole. CLANG.

Back and forth. Back and forth. Neither one of them could win. They were exactly the same strength. Every punch Thanos threw, Bruce threw one back. Every time Bruce knocked Thanos down, Thanos got right back up.

They stood facing each other on the torn-up grass. Both of them breathing hard.

"You are stronger than before," said Thanos. "I'll give you that."

"Told you," said Bruce.

"But it does not matter," said Thanos. "When I have my stones back, I will finish what I started. I will wipe this little planet clean. Every city. Every field. Every pesky little human." He looked past Bruce, at the Compound. "Starting with the ones in that building."

Bruce went very still.

He thought about the people in that building.

He thought about Tim, who tripped over everything and never gave up. He thought about Lindsey, who found him in a building with a hundred rooms when nobody else could. He thought about Lainey, racing everybody, and Sydney, untangling knots that nobody else could see.

And he thought about Paisley.

Paisley, the very first day, taking one small step forward in a tiny lab full of green goo, with her knees shaking, when everyone else was frozen. Paisley breathing with him. Paisley holding his hand on the curb in New York when the Hulk wouldn't come out. Paisley on the edge of a giant hot tub on another planet, saying Crown Girl, that's me. Paisley on the rug at the Compound, saying, you can do them together.

And he thought about Thanos's giant purple hand reaching for her.

Something deep inside Bruce went BOOM.

It wasn't the old Hulk anger. The scared kind. The kind that smashed everything and didn't know why. This was something new. Something bigger. It was the kind of feeling you get when someone wants to hurt the people you love. And you will not let them. Not ever.

Bruce's green skin glowed brighter. His muscles grew. His eyes, behind his glasses, burned.

"No," he said. His voice was so deep it made the ground hum. "Not. Them."

He hit Thanos.

WHAAAAAM!

Thanos flew backward across the whole lawn. He bounced off the ground once. Twice. Three times. He skidded through the grass and crashed into the side of the parking lot so hard the cars jumped.

He got up. He shook his head. He was angry now. Really angry.

He charged at Bruce with his biggest, strongest punch. The one that had knocked out the Hulk in New Asgard. The one that had never, ever missed.

Bruce caught it.

He caught Thanos's giant fist in his giant green hand. And he held it. Thanos pushed. Bruce didn't move. Thanos pushed harder, so hard his whole body shook. Bruce didn't move one inch.

Then Bruce squeezed.

Thanos's knees bent. He sank down, lower and lower, until he was kneeling in the grass. He looked up at Bruce. His eyes were very wide.

"How?" said Thanos.

"I've got a lot of friends in that building," said Bruce. "You picked the wrong ones to threaten."

He let go of Thanos's fist. Then he grabbed Thanos by the ankle.

And just like he did to Loki, a long time ago, on the top of Stark Tower, Bruce swung him. Up over his head. WHUMP, onto the grass on one side. Up over his head again. WHUMP, onto the grass on the other side. WHUMP. WHUMP. WHUMP. Back and forth, back and forth, like a big purple rag doll.

Then he spun around in a circle, faster and faster, and let go.

Thanos went flying. All the way across the field. He crashed right through the front line of his own army and knocked over a whole row of outriders like bowling pins. BOOM.

The outriders looked at their leader, lying in the dirt. Then they looked at the big green man walking toward them. A few of them started to back away.

Thanos climbed to his feet. His golden armor was dented and scraped. He grabbed a giant rock from the ground, as big as a car, and threw it.

Bruce punched it. The rock exploded into gravel.

Thanos ran at him, swinging both fists. Bruce ducked under the left one. He blocked the right one. Then he hit Thanos with an uppercut so hard that Thanos's feet left the ground. He flew straight up, spinning, and came straight back down, CRASH, flat on his back.

Bruce grabbed the front of Thanos's golden chest armor. He ripped it right off. Thanos's big gold armor plate, the one that had never once been scratched. Bruce held it up. He looked at it. Then he folded it in half, like a piece of paper. CRRRUNCH. And tossed it over his shoulder.

Thanos stared at it. He started crawling backward through the grass, away from Bruce. Thanos. The strongest, scariest being in the whole universe. Crawling backward.

"Impossible," he said. "You were nothing. I beat you."

"You beat me when I was scared," said Bruce. "And when I was fighting by myself." He cracked his knuckles. "I'm not scared anymore. And I'm never by myself."

He lifted Thanos up by his collar, high off the ground, so his feet dangled.

"Puny Titan," said Bruce.

Back at the Compound, faces were pressed against every window that hadn't been broken. Lainey was jumping up and down. Sydney had stopped drawing, with her pencil frozen in the air. Thor had both fists in the air and was yelling, "YES! YES! THAT'S MY FRIEND! THAT'S MY FRIEND!"

And Paisley was standing on a chair, cheering louder than anybody. "GO, BRUCE! GO, HULK! USE YOUR BIG FEELINGS!"

Bruce was winning. Not just a little. Not almost. He was really, truly winning. One more punch and the fight would be over.

Thanos knew it too.

Dangling from Bruce's fist, he raised one hand toward the sky. And he made a signal to his ship.

High above them, the giant ship opened its cannons.

They were long and black, pointing down from the bottom of the giant metal flower like the stems of upside-down weeds. Thanos's soldiers on the ship had seen their leader's signal. And every single cannon was pointed at the Avengers Compound.

BOOOM. BOOOM. BOOOM.

The cannons fired. The blasts hit the roof of the Compound. The whole building groaned. Cracks ran down the walls like lightning. Big chunks of the roof started to fall.

Bruce's star pin crackled. It was Lindsey.

"Bruce! We're in the tunnel! But the floor above us is cracking! The ceiling's coming down over the stairs! Not everyone is through yet!"

Bruce looked at Thanos, dangling from his fist. One more punch. That's all it would take. He could finish this. Right now.

Then he looked at the building. Falling apart. With his friends underneath it.

He didn't even have to think about it.

He dropped Thanos in the grass. And he turned and ran.

He crashed through the front door of the Compound. Everything was shaking. Dust was falling. The giant metal beam that held up the middle of the whole building was bending, creaking, coming down right over the staircase that led to the tunnel.

At the bottom of the stairs, he could see them. Steve, waving people through. Natasha, carrying a box of supplies. Lindsey, holding up her map. Tim, counting. And the girls.

The beam cracked. It started to fall.

Bruce jumped under it. He caught it on his shoulders.

CRUNCH.

The whole weight of the roof came down on him. Tons and tons of metal and concrete and glass. His knees bent. His arms shook. But he held it.

"GO!" roared Bruce. "Everybody GO! I've got it!"

They went. Down the stairs and into the tunnel. Rhodey. Sam. Bucky. Wanda and Vision. The Guardians. Scott and Hope and Cassie. Tony, carrying Peter, who had hurt his ankle. Thor, carrying three people at once.

Tim stood at the bottom of the stairs, counting every single one.

"Lainey! Sydney! Lindsey!" He looked around. "Paisley?"

Paisley was at the top of the stairs. Right under Bruce. Looking up at him, holding the whole building on his back.

"Paisley, go," said Bruce through gritted teeth. "Go with your dad."

"Not without you," said Paisley.

"I'm right behind you," said Bruce. "I promise. Nobody gets left behind. Right?"

Paisley looked at him for one more second. Then she ran down the stairs and jumped into Tim's arms.

"That's everybody," said Tim, his voice shaking. "Bruce! That's everybody!"

Bruce let go.

He dove down the stairs just as the roof came crashing down behind him. BOOOOOOM. Dust and smoke blew down the tunnel like a giant sneeze. The little blue lights flickered.

Then it was quiet.

They ran and ran. The tunnel went on and on, under the lawn, under the parking lot, all the way out to the edge of the woods. They climbed up a ladder and out of a little hatch in the trees. Everyone. Tim counted again. Every single person.

Bruce came out last. He was covered in dust from head to toe. His glasses were crooked. He was so tired he could barely stand. He sat down hard on a log.

Paisley ran to him and threw her arms around his giant green neck.

"You were winning," she said. "You were really winning. And you stopped."

"Yeah," said Bruce. He was breathing very hard.

"Why?"

Bruce looked down at her. He smiled a tired, dusty smile.

"Because winning a fight doesn't matter," he said, "if the people you're fighting for aren't there anymore."

Paisley hugged him even tighter.

Then they all turned around and looked.

The Compound was gone. The big white building with all the glass windows, where they had eaten spaghetti just two nights before, where Vision had read every book in the library, where Tony had built the glove. It was a giant pile of broken stones and smoke.

But nobody was inside it. Bruce had made sure.

"How did you know about the tunnels?" asked Tony, staring at Lindsey.

"I read the building plans," said Lindsey. "The first week you moved in. I always read the building plans."

"Of course you do," said Tony.

Thor put one hand on Bruce's big dusty shoulder. "My friend," he said. "You had him. I saw it. You had him dangling like a fish on a hook."

"I'll get another chance," said Bruce. He tried to stand up. His legs wobbled. He sat back down. "Just... maybe not for a few minutes. Holding up a building is really, really hard."

"Then rest," said Steve. "We'll take it from here."

Then they walked out of the woods. Onto the big open field where the Compound used to be.

And there, waiting for them, was Thanos.

He stood in the middle of the field. His golden chest armor was gone, still lying folded in half somewhere on the lawn. His arms and shoulders were scraped and dented. Behind him, the army was coming down from the giant ship. Outriders, running on all their legs. Flying ships, swooping and diving. Huge creatures as big as buildings, with armored backs and long tails. More and more and more of them, filling up the whole field, all the way to the trees.

Thousands. Maybe millions.

Thanos looked at them. He didn't smile. He didn't frown.

"Give me the stones," he said. "And I will leave this little planet alone."

"No," said Steve.

"Then I will take them," said Thanos.

Three of them stepped forward to meet him. Steve, with his shield. Tony, in his red and gold suit. And Thor, with Stormbreaker in one hand and Mjolnir in the other.

"Stay back," Steve said to the family. "Keep the box safe. Keep each other safe."

"Nobody gets left behind," said Tim.

"Nobody gets left behind," said Steve.

The three of them walked across the field toward Thanos.

The fight was big. The biggest fight anyone had ever seen.

Tony flew in circles around Thanos, firing beams from his hands. Thor called down lightning from the sky, crack after crack after crack. Steve threw his shield, and it bounced off Thanos's armor, CLANG, and came back to his hand.

But Thanos was so strong. He didn't need the stones to be strong. He grabbed Tony out of the air and threw him into the ground. He knocked Steve's shield out of his hands. He grabbed Stormbreaker and wrestled with Thor, pushing him down, down, down, until Thor was on his knees.

Mjolnir fell out of Thor's other hand. It landed in the grass.

It landed right next to Steve.

Steve was lying on his back. He was hurt. He was tired. His shield was far away. Thanos was about to hit Thor with his own ax.

Steve looked at Mjolnir, lying in the grass beside him.

He reached out his hand.

At the edge of the field, Sydney grabbed Lindsey's arm. "Mom," she whispered. "Mom, look. Look at Steve."

Mjolnir moved.

It wiggled. Like it did at Tony's party, all that time ago.

Then it rose up off the grass. And it flew. Straight into Steve's hand.

Steve stood up, holding Thor's hammer.

Lightning crackled all around him. His eyes glowed white. Blue sparks ran up and down his arm.

Thor, on his knees under Thanos's ax, looked over and saw it. His mouth fell open. And then he shouted, with a big happy grin all over his tired face:

"I KNEW IT!"

"I KNEW IT TOO!" screamed Paisley from the edge of the field.

Steve swung Mjolnir. It hit Thanos right in the chest. KA-BOOOOOM! Thanos went flying backward across the field. Lightning exploded everywhere.

Sydney opened her notebook with shaking hands. She found the page that said WORTHY. Under VISION (on his very first day!!!), she wrote:

STEVE. (We always knew.)

Steve threw Mjolnir. It flew through the air, smashing through a line of outriders. Then he caught his shield on the bounce, and Mjolnir came back to his other hand, and he stood there with both of them, the hammer and the shield, glowing with lightning.

But the army was coming. All of it. A huge wave of outriders and monsters and flying ships, pouring across the field toward them.

Thor was hurt. Tony was hurt. Steve was alone in the middle of the field. One man, with a shield and a hammer, against millions.

He looked at the army. He tightened his grip on his shield.

And he didn't run.

Then Tim's star pin crackled. A voice came through. A voice they all knew.

"Hey, Cap," said Sam. "On your left."

Steve turned his head.

Behind him, in the air, a little circle of orange sparks appeared. Then it got bigger. And bigger. And opened up.

Through it flew Sam, on his silver wings.

Then another circle opened. And another. And another. All over the field. Dozens of them. Hundreds. Every one of them made of glowing orange sparks.

Through one came T'Challa and Shuri and Okoye, leading the whole army of Wakanda. Warriors with glowing shields. War rhinos with armor on their backs. M'Baku and his mountain warriors, shouting "HOO! HOO! HOO!"

Through another came Doctor Strange and Wong and all the sorcerers of Kamar-Taj, their hands full of magic.

Through another came the Asgardians of New Asgard, with Valkyrie at the front riding a flying white horse with big feathery wings. Korg was beside her, holding a big rock hammer. Loki was there too, in his golden horned helmet, looking very surprised to be included.

Through another came the Nova Corps. Hundreds of little round ships from Xandar, flying in perfect lines.

"Xandar's okay!" shouted Paisley. "The Nova Corps are okay!"

Through another came Pepper, Tony's fiancée, in her own suit of blue and silver armor. Through another came Peter Parker, swinging in on a web. Through another came Bucky and Wanda and Vision, with his new silver heart glowing in his chest. Right behind them came a blue blur that skidded to a stop and turned into Pietro, grinning. Through another came Hope in her Wasp suit, and Scott, and Rocket and Groot and Quill and Gamora and Nebula and Drax and Mantis.

And through one more came a whole group of people in mismatched space armor, riding big clunky ships. They were Ravagers, Quill's old space pirate friends, yelling and whooping.

The whole field filled up. Everyone who had ever helped. Everyone who had ever been helped. Standing together. Facing Thanos's army.

Steve looked around at all of them.

Then he raised Mjolnir up toward the sky. Lightning crackled down and hit it.

"Avengers," said Steve. Then, louder, so everyone could hear:

"ASSEMBLE!"

And everyone charged.

At the edge of the woods, a group of Wakandan warriors had gotten hurt. Not badly. But they couldn't fight anymore, and they couldn't walk very fast, and the outriders were getting close.

Lindsey looked at her map. She looked at the woods.

"There's a little stream behind those trees," she said. "With a big rock overhang. Like a cave. The outriders won't see it from the field. Tim, help them. I'll show you the way."

So Tim and Lindsey led them. Tim put his shoulder under one warrior's arm and helped him walk. Lindsey went first, holding branches out of the way. Paisley carried a warrior's helmet, which was so big it covered her whole head, so she had to walk with one hand out in front of her like a zombie.

"I can't see!" said Paisley.

"Turn left," said Lindsey. "Now straight. Now duck."

Paisley ducked. A branch went swish over her head.

They got every single warrior into the little cave behind the stream. Tim counted them twice.

"Everybody's here," he said.

One of the warriors, an older woman with gray in her braids, took Tim's hand. "Thank you," she said. "You are not a warrior. But you fight like one."

Tim didn't know what to say. So he just said, "Nobody gets left behind."

The woman smiled. "Yes," she said. "That is how warriors fight."

Then the family ran back to the edge of the field. Because they had a job. They always had a job.

"The glove has to get to the middle of the field," said Lindsey. She was standing on top of a big rock at the edge of the woods, holding her map of the battlefield, which she had drawn in about thirty seconds. "Right here. By the old flagpole. That's where everyone will meet. That's where everyone will hold hands."

"Why there?" asked Tim.

"Because it's the one spot that everybody can get to," said Lindsey. "From every direction. It's the middle. Like the middle bead on a string."

Tim pressed his star pin. "Everyone! When it's time, meet at the flagpole! In the middle of the field! Everyone!"

All over the battlefield, heroes heard him.

"But first," said Tony, landing next to them with a CLANK, "we have to get the glove there. And Thanos knows. He's going to try to take it."

Tony took the red and gold glove out of the vibranium box. The six stones glowed in it so brightly that everyone had to squint.

At the other end of the field, Thanos turned his head. He saw the glow.

He started walking toward them. His army turned around and followed.

"Okay," said Tony. "Here we go. We pass it. Like a football. Nobody holds it too long."

He took off, flying low over the field with the glove. Outriders jumped at him. He dodged them. A giant creature swung its tail. He flew under it.

But Thanos's flying ships came after him. Too many of them. They surrounded him.

"Peter!" yelled Tony.

He threw the glove. Peter Parker shot a web, caught it, and swung away with it. THWIP. THWIP.

The outriders chased Peter. He swung over them. He swung under them. Then a monster grabbed his leg.

"Help!" yelled Peter. "Somebody!"

Valkyrie swooped down on her flying horse and snatched the glove out of his hand. She flew up into the sky.

A flying ship blasted her horse's wing. They tumbled. Valkyrie threw the glove as hard as she could.

It fell. Spinning. Glowing. Down toward the field.

It landed in the grass.

Right in front of Lainey.

Lainey looked at it. She looked up.

Between her and the flagpole was the whole battlefield. Outriders. Monsters. Smoke. Fire. And Thanos, walking toward her, getting closer with every giant step.

"Lainey," said Lindsey, very quietly. "Sweetheart."

Lainey picked up the glove. It was heavy. It was warm. It hummed in her hands like a hive full of bees.

She looked back at her family. Tim. Lindsey. Sydney. Paisley.

"I can do it," she said.

Tim knelt down. He put his hands on her shoulders.

"I know you can," he said. "You're the fastest runner anyone has ever seen."

Lainey ran.

She remembered what Okoye told her on the mountain. A warrior doesn't run away. A warrior runs toward something. Not to get away. To get there.

So she didn't run away from the outriders. She ran toward the flagpole. Toward Tony. Toward everybody waiting with their hands out.

She ran across the field. She ran through the smoke. She ran past an outrider, and it jumped at her, and she zigged. She ran past another, and it jumped too, and she zagged.

But there were too many of them. A whole wall of outriders turned toward her. Dozens of them. Then hundreds. All of them running at one little girl in sneakers, holding a glowing glove.

Then a voice behind her said, "Oh, honestly. Let me help."

There was a flash of green light.

And suddenly, Lainey wasn't one Lainey anymore.

There were twenty of her. Twenty Laineys, all in the same sneakers, all holding the same glowing glove, all running in twenty different directions across the battlefield.

The outriders skidded to a stop. They looked left. They looked right. They looked at all the Laineys. They didn't know which one to chase.

So they chased all of them. In twenty different directions. Crashing into each other. Tripping over each other. Biting each other's tails by mistake.

Lainey looked over her shoulder. Standing on a rock at the edge of the field, with his hands glowing green and a very pleased smile on his face, was Loki.

"LOKI!" yelled Lainey, still running. "THAT'S SO COOL!"

"I know," Loki called back. "Keep running. They'll figure it out eventually. They're not very smart."

At the edge of the woods, Lindsey watched twenty Laineys running across the battlefield. She pulled out her map with shaking hands. She looked at the front side. LOKI = TROUBLE. ...but maybe not ALL trouble. ...still watching.

She took out her pen. And right there, in the middle of the biggest battle in the history of the world, she drew a line through LOKI = TROUBLE.

And underneath it she wrote: LOKI = FRIEND.

And then she looked up. And she saw she wasn't alone.

Above her, low over the grass, Carol Danvers was flying. Glowing gold. Flying right beside her, keeping pace, protecting her from anything that came close.

On her other side, Peter was swinging. THWIP. THWIP. Right beside her.

Behind her, Sam was flying on his silver wings. And Pietro, Wanda's brother, the fastest man in the world, was running. A blue blur, right at her side.

And Lainey was keeping up.

Not almost. Not nearly. Not by a little bit.

She was keeping up. With all of them.

She ran faster than she had ever run in her whole life. Faster than Sleipnir. Faster than Steve. Faster than the maglev train in Wakanda. Her feet barely touched the ground. The wind roared in her ears. Outriders jumped and missed. Monsters swung and missed. Nothing could catch her.

Pietro looked over at her and grinned. "Little Rocket!" he shouted. "You're keeping up!"

"I KNOW!" yelled Lainey.

Carol looked down at her. Carol, the strongest person in the universe, who could fly through stars. She was smiling.

"Kid," she said, "you're FAST."

Lainey ran all the way to the flagpole in the middle of the field. And she stopped. And she held up the glowing glove.

"I DID IT!" she screamed. "I KEPT UP! I KEPT UP!"

Tony landed right next to her. CLANK. He took the glove from her hands. Then he knelt down and hugged her, metal suit and all.

"You sure did, Little Rocket," he said. "You sure did."

But Thanos had followed her.

He was right there. At the edge of the circle around the flagpole. Huge and purple and angry. His army behind him.

"Give it to me," he growled.

Wanda stepped in front of him. Red magic poured out of her hands. It wrapped around Thanos like a giant red rope. He pushed against it. It held.

"I'm not letting you near them," said Wanda.

Carol flew straight up into the sky. Up, up, up, toward the giant spiky ship hanging over the field. She glowed so bright she looked like a sun. Then she flew right through the middle of it.

KA-BOOOOOOM!

The giant ship broke apart into a thousand pieces. All of Thanos's soldiers had already come down to the field. The ship was empty. It fell slowly out of the sky in big chunks, crashing into the lake far away.

Thanos looked up at it. For the first time, he looked worried.

Tony put on the glove.

The six stones snapped into place against his hand.

And the power hit him.

It was like being hit by every lightning bolt in the world at once. Light poured up his arm. His whole body started glowing. His suit sparked and smoked. He fell to one knee.

"It's too much," Tony gasped. "It's too much. I can't. I can't hold it."

Bruce, big and green, started to reach for him.

And then Sydney ran into the circle.

"HOLD HANDS!" she shouted, at the top of her lungs. "EVERYBODY! HOLD HANDS! Like Xandar! It's too much for one person! But not for all of us!"

She grabbed Tony's free hand.

The light poured into her. It was hot and huge and buzzy. Her hair stood straight up. But Tony's face got a little better.

"Lindsey!" yelled Sydney.

Lindsey grabbed Sydney's other hand. Then Tim grabbed Lindsey's. Then Lainey grabbed Tim's. Then Paisley grabbed Lainey's.
